the coefficientwise class answers, with a common well-ordered support
\emph{assumed}, part of source~10's question about a weaker addition rule with
convergent real coefficient sums (Question~\ref{meas:q:weaker}).

This requirement is natural when a surreal quantity records a hierarchy of exact
asymptotic corrections to an ordinary probabilistic model: the leading
coefficient is the ordinary law, later coefficients are successively smaller
corrections, and finite signed coefficient measures permit integration,
conditioning on events of nonzero probability and coefficientwise limits by
ordinary measure theory. They do not require a strong sum of every countable
family of event probabilities.

\section{The signed extension test and null-ideal positivity}
\label{meas:sec:coef-extension}

\subsection{Uniform total variation is the exact signed extension test}

Let $E$ be a nonempty finite alphabet, $\Omega=E^{\N}$ with its compact metric
product topology, and $\pi_n:\Omega\to E^n$ the first-$n$ projection. A cylinder
function depends on finitely many coordinates; these continuous functions are
uniformly dense in $C(\Omega)$. Finite signed or complex Borel measures on the
compact metric space $\Omega$ are automatically regular. Suppose
$\lambda_n(x)\in K$, $x\in E^n$, are consistent finite-dimensional weights whose
supports all lie in one well-ordered $W$, and put
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:variation}
 c_{\gamma,n}(x)=\coef{\gamma}{\lambda_n(x)},\qquad
 V_\gamma(n)=\sum_{x\in E^n}\abs{c_{\gamma,n}(x)}.
\end{equation}

\begin{theorem}[Coefficientwise signed extension test; source 15]
\label{meas:thm:extension}
There is a coefficientwise Hahn measure on $\Omega$ whose pushforwards under the
$\pi_n$ have weights $\lambda_n$ if and only if
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:var-test}
 \sup_{n\ge1}V_\gamma(n)<\infty\qquad\text{for every }\gamma\in W.
\end{equation}
The extension is unique, its coefficient support is contained in $W$, and
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:norm-exact}
 \norm{\mu_\gamma}_\TV=\sup_nV_\gamma(n).
\end{equation}
This is an application of the classical bounded-functional representation of
finite measures, not a new scalar extension theorem.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
If an extension exists, pushforward does not increase total variation, which
gives necessity and $V_\gamma(n)\le\norm{\mu_\gamma}_\TV$. For sufficiency fix
$\gamma$ and define, for a cylinder function $f=g\circ\pi_n$,
$L_\gamma(f)=\sum_{x\in E^n}g(x)c_{\gamma,n}(x)$. Consistency makes this independent
of the level. Its norm on cylinder functions is exactly $\sup_nV_\gamma(n)$: the
upper bound is immediate, and equality at level $n$ is attained by choosing the
sign, or complex phase, of each coefficient. It extends uniquely by uniform
density to a bounded functional on $C(\Omega)$, and the Riesz--Markov theorem
gives a unique finite regular signed or complex measure $\mu_\gamma$ with that
functional and norm. Do this for each $\gamma\in W$, set the other coefficients
to zero, and use \eqref{meas:eq:coef-measure}. A coefficient outside $W$ of any
competing extension vanishes on all cylinders, hence is zero by uniqueness, and
the same uniqueness at each exponent proves uniqueness of the coefficientwise
Hahn measure.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The transfinite null-ideal positivity criterion}

Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:extension} is a signed statement: nonnegativity of
the full Hahn-valued cylinder masses need not pass to arbitrary Borel sets.
The leading scalar coefficient is different. If it is nonnegative on
cylinders, then it is nonnegative on finite unions of cylinders, and
approximation in its finite total variation makes it a positive Borel
measure. Later coefficients may still place negative mass on its null sets,
as in Example~\ref{meas:ex:negative}. The following criterion decides
positivity of the full Hahn-valued measure. It is stated on an arbitrary
measurable
space; its proof uses no topology, no countability of $W$ and no divisibility of
$\Gamma$. For a finite signed measure $\nu$ write $\nu^+,\nu^-$ for its Jordan
parts and $\abs\nu=\nu^++\nu^-$.

\begin{theorem}[Null-ideal positivity criterion; source 15, and independently
source 18]\label{meas:thm:nullideal}
Let $(X,\Sigma)$ be a measurable space, $W\subseteq\Gamma$ a well-ordered set of
any cardinality, and $(\mu_\gamma)_{\gamma\in W}$ finite real signed measures on
$\Sigma$. Put $\mu(A)=\sum_{\gamma\in W}\mu_\gamma(A)t^\gamma\in\KR$ and, for
$\gamma\in W$,
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:nullideal}
 \mathcal J_{<\gamma}=\bigl\{A\in\Sigma:\abs{\mu_\delta}(A)=0
 \text{ for every }\delta\in W\text{ with }\delta<\gamma\bigr\},
\end{equation}
a $\sigma$-ideal of $\Sigma$ ($\mathcal J_{<\min W}=\Sigma$). The following are
equivalent.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item $\mu(A)\ge0$ for every $A\in\Sigma$.
\item For every $\gamma\in W$ and $A\in\Sigma$:
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:null-criterion}
 \bigl[\abs{\mu_\delta}(A)=0\ \text{for every }\delta<\gamma\bigr]
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \mu_\gamma(A)\ge0 .
\end{equation}
\item $\mu_\gamma^-(A)=0$ for every $\gamma\in W$ and every $A\in\mathcal J_{<\gamma}$.
\end{enumerate}
If the predecessor set $\{\delta\in W:\delta<\gamma\}$ is countable, enumerate it as
$(\beta_j)$ (finitely or countably) and put
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:control}
 \lambda_{<\gamma}=\sum_j2^{-j}\frac{\abs{\mu_{\beta_j}}}{1+\norm{\mu_{\beta_j}}_\TV},
\end{equation}
with $\lambda_{<\gamma}=0$ for an empty predecessor set. Then the condition at
$\gamma$ in (c) is equivalent to $\mu_\gamma^-\ll\lambda_{<\gamma}$. No bound on a
Radon--Nikodym derivative is required.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
(a)$\Rightarrow$(b): under the hypothesis of \eqref{meas:eq:null-criterion} every
coefficient of $\mu(A)$ before $\gamma$ vanishes, so a negative $\mu_\gamma(A)$
would be a negative leading coefficient.
(b)$\Rightarrow$(c): $\mathcal J_{<\gamma}$ is closed under measurable subsets, so
(b) gives $\mu_\gamma(B)\ge0$ for every measurable $B\subseteq A\in\mathcal
J_{<\gamma}$, whence $\mu_\gamma^-(A)=0$. (c)$\Rightarrow$(b):
$\mu_\gamma(A)=\mu_\gamma^+(A)-\mu_\gamma^-(A)=\mu_\gamma^+(A)\ge0$ for
$A\in\mathcal J_{<\gamma}$.

(b)$\Rightarrow$(a): take $A$ with $\mu(A)\ne0$ and let $\gamma$ be the least
exponent with $\mu_\gamma(A)\ne0$. We prove by transfinite induction on
$\delta\in W$, $\delta<\gamma$, that $\abs{\mu_\delta}(A)=0$. If this holds at all
earlier indices, every measurable $B\subseteq A$ is null for those earlier total
variations, and \eqref{meas:eq:null-criterion} gives $\mu_\delta(B)\ge0$. So the
restriction of $\mu_\delta$ to $A$ is a positive measure of total mass
$\mu_\delta(A)=0$; it is zero, and so is $\abs{\mu_\delta}(A)$. Limit stages need
nothing, since no measure is summed over the earlier indices. Now
\eqref{meas:eq:null-criterion} at $\gamma$ gives $\mu_\gamma(A)\ge0$, and it is
nonzero, hence positive; this is positivity of $\mu(A)$ in the Hahn order.

Finally, \eqref{meas:eq:control} is a finite positive measure (total mass at most
$1$) whose null sets are exactly the common null sets of the $\abs{\mu_{\beta_j}}$,
since every weight is strictly positive; that is, its null sets form
$\mathcal J_{<\gamma}$, and (c) at $\gamma$ is $\mu_\gamma^-\ll\lambda_{<\gamma}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Provenance and scope of the criterion]\label{meas:rem:nullideal}
Source~15 stated the equivalence (a)$\Leftrightarrow$(b) for finite signed Borel
measures on a compact metric space, with the proof above. Source~18 of the same
batch, now the report \path{surcomplex/hahn-herglotz-positivity}~\cite{repoherg},
proved (a)$\Leftrightarrow$(c) and the control-measure form independently
(\texttt{herg:thm:nullideal}), for
real finite signed regular Borel measures on a compact Hausdorff space, using a
Hahn decomposition for the necessity half; its standing hypothesis that
$\Gamma$ be divisible is not used in that proof. Both
proofs work verbatim on any measurable space and any $\Gamma$, and the statement
above covers both settings. The countable control measure is a corollary, not a
substitute: source~18 gives (\texttt{herg:ex:uncountable}) a positive measure
with uncountable well-ordered
support, earlier coefficients Dirac masses and a final diffuse negative
coefficient, for which $\mu_\gamma^-\ll\lambda$ fails for every control measure
$\lambda$ built from countably many earlier coefficients; so the full null ideals
are needed when predecessor sets are uncountable. For two exponents,
$W=\{0,\eta\}$ with $\eta>0$, the criterion reads: $\mu_0+t^\eta\mu_\eta\ge0$ if and
only if $\mu_0\ge0$ and $\mu_\eta^-\ll\mu_0$. The criterion permits negative
coefficient measures (for an ordinary probability $P$, $P+t^\eta g\,P$ is positive
for every real $g\in L^1(P)$, however negative or unbounded); it excludes a
negative component concentrated on a set invisible to all earlier coefficients.
The Herglotz report finds such a hidden negative atom already at two exponents
on the circle (\texttt{herg:thm:negativeatom}).
No matrix-valued version is claimed. For countable $W$ one control serves all
exponents at once, and the criterion becomes the pointwise inequality
$\theta\ge0$ for a Hahn-valued density (source~16;
Theorem~\ref{meas:cw:thm:representation} and
Proposition~\ref{meas:cw:prop:nullideal}).
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[Dominated corrections; source 15]\label{meas:cor:dominated}
Let $\mu$ be a real coefficientwise Hahn measure with $0\in W\subseteq\Gamma_{\ge0}$,
$\mu_0=P_0$ an ordinary probability, and $\mu_\gamma\ll P_0$ for every $\gamma>0$.
Then $\mu(A)\ge0$ for every event $A$, and $\mu(A)=0$ if and only if $P_0(A)=0$.
If in addition $\mu_\gamma(X)=0$ for $\gamma>0$, then $\mu$ is a coefficientwise Hahn
probability.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
If $P_0(A)>0$ it is the positive leading coefficient. If $P_0(A)=0$, absolute
continuity makes every coefficient vanish. Normalization is coefficientwise.
\end{proof}

\begin{example}[Positive cylinders, negative singleton, two exponents;
source 15]\label{meas:ex:negative}
Let $P_0$ be the ordinary fair Bernoulli measure on $\{0,1\}^{\N}$, choose distinct
sequences $x,y$, let $\alpha>0$, and put
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:bad-positive}
 \mu=P_0+t^\alpha(\delta_y-\delta_x).
\end{equation}
Every nonempty event depending on finitely many coordinates has strictly
positive $P_0$-probability, so every finite-dimensional marginal of $\mu$ is a
positive normalized Hahn law; the marginals are consistent and their
$\alpha$-coefficient variations are bounded by $2$. Nonetheless
$\mu(\{x\})=-t^\alpha<0$, since $P_0$ is atomless. By
Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:extension} no other coefficientwise extension of these
marginals exists, positive or not. In the language of
Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:nullideal}: $\{x\}\in\mathcal J_{<\alpha}$, but
$\mu_\alpha^-(\{x\})=1$.
\end{example}

The support $\{0,\alpha\}$ has order type $2$; the sample space and the tests are
those of Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:threshold}(a). This is Trap~1 of
Section~\ref{meas:sub:traps}: the measure is not strong, because its leading
coefficient is atomless. The Herglotz report~\cite{repoherg} gives the
corresponding two-exponent example with Toeplitz tests on the circle.

\begin{remark}[One exponent never suffices]\label{meas:rem:one-exponent}
With a single exponent the phenomenon cannot occur in either class: if
$\mu=\mu_0$ is an ordinary finite signed measure on the Borel sets of Cantor space
and $\mu_0([s])\ge0$ for every cylinder, then $\mu_0\ge0$, because every Borel
set $B$ is approximated by finite unions $A$ of cylinders with
$\abs{\mu_0}(A\triangle B)$ arbitrarily small. So the least support order type
admitting nonnegative cylinders and a negative singleton is $2$ in the
coefficientwise class, against $\omega+1$ in the strong class. (This elementary
remark is added in the merge.)
\end{remark}

\section{Interior Bernoulli laws: the exact square-summability theorem}
\label{meas:sec:interior}

Let $S\subseteq\Gamma_{>0}$ be well ordered, and write
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:perturbation}
 p_j=p_j^0+\epsilon_j,\qquad \epsilon_j=\sum_{\gamma\in S}a_{j,\gamma}t^\gamma,
\end{equation}
with the \emph{same} $S$ for all coordinates $j$. The \emph{row} at $\gamma$ is the
sequence $(a_{j,\gamma})_{j\ge1}$. Let $0<\eta\le\frac12$, $p_j^0\in[\eta,1-\eta]$ and
$q_j^0=1-p_j^0$; every $p_j$ lies strictly between $0$ and $1$ in $\KR$. Let $P_n$
be the finite independent law \eqref{meas:eq:finite-intro} with these biases, and
$P_0$ the ordinary infinite product with coordinate biases $p_j^0$.

\Needspace{14\baselineskip}
\begin{theorem}[Interior extension criterion; source 15]
\label{meas:thm:interior}
The following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item the finite laws $P_n$ admit a coefficientwise signed Hahn extension;
\item for every $\gamma\in S$ the row
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:b-row}
 b_\gamma=(b_{j,\gamma})_{j\ge1},\qquad b_{j,\gamma}=\frac{a_{j,\gamma}}{\sqrt{p_j^0q_j^0}},
\end{equation}
belongs to $\ell^2$.
\end{enumerate}
When they hold the extension is unique and is a coefficientwise Hahn
probability. Its coefficients are supported in $S^*$, its constant coefficient
is $P_0$, and
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:density-measure}
 \mu_\rho(A)=\int_AH_\rho\,dP_0,\qquad H_\rho\in L^2(P_0).
\end{equation}
Its null Borel events are exactly the $P_0$-null events. No bound on
$\norm{b_\gamma}_2$ uniform in $\gamma$ is required.
\end{theorem}

\subsection{Orthogonal multilinear coefficients}

Under $P_0$ put $Z_j=(X_j-p_j^0)/\sqrt{p_j^0q_j^0}$: independent, centered, of
variance one. For finite $I\subseteq\N$ put $Z_I=\prod_{j\in I}Z_j$, $Z_\varnothing=1$;
then
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:orthogonality}
 \E_0[Z_IZ_J]=\ind_{I=J}.
\end{equation}
For $k\ge1$ and $u_1,\ldots,u_k\in\ell^2$ set
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:Q}
 Q_n(u_1,\ldots,u_k)=\frac1{k!}
 \sum_{\substack{j_1,\ldots,j_k\le n\\\text{pairwise distinct}}}
 \Bigl(\prod_{r=1}^ku_r(j_r)\Bigr)Z_{j_1}\cdots Z_{j_k}.
\end{equation}

\begin{lemma}[Multilinear chaos bound; source 15]\label{meas:lem:chaos}
$Q_n(u_1,\ldots,u_k)$ converges in $L^2(P_0)$ to a multilinear $Q(u_1,\ldots,u_k)$,
and
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:chaos-bound}
 \norm{Q(u_1,\ldots,u_k)}_2\le\frac1{\sqrt{k!}}\prod_{r=1}^k\norm{u_r}_2 ;
\end{equation}
the same bound holds for $Q_n$, and conditional expectation onto the first $n$
coordinates sends $Q$ to $Q_n$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Group \eqref{meas:eq:Q} by the unordered set $I=\{j_1,\ldots,j_k\}$: the coefficient
of $Z_I$ is the average of the $k!$ corresponding products. The square of an
average is at most the average of the squares, so by
\eqref{meas:eq:orthogonality}
\[
 \norm{Q_n(u_1,\ldots,u_k)}_2^2\le\frac1{k!}
 \sum_{\substack{j_1,\ldots,j_k\le n\\\text{pairwise distinct}}}
 \prod_{r=1}^k\abs{u_r(j_r)}^2\le\frac1{k!}\prod_{r=1}^k\norm{u_r}_2^2 .
\]
For finitely supported $u_r$, $Q_n$ eventually stabilizes. For general $u_r$,
approximate by truncations and use multilinearity: a difference of two products
is a sum of $k$ multilinear terms each containing one tail, which the bound makes
small in $L^2$ uniformly in $n$. A term $Z_I$ with an index beyond $n$ has zero
conditional expectation onto the first $n$ coordinates, which proves the last
claim for finitely supported inputs, and $L^2$ continuity gives the general case.
\end{proof}

The finite likelihood ratio relative to $P_0$ is
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:likelihood}
 L_n=\prod_{j=1}^n\Bigl(1+Z_j\sum_{\gamma\in S}b_{j,\gamma}t^\gamma\Bigr),
\end{equation}
since the one-coordinate ratio is $1+\epsilon_j/p_j^0$ at $X_j=1$ and
$1-\epsilon_j/q_j^0$ at $X_j=0$. At a positive exponent $\rho$,
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:H-finite}
 H_{\rho,n}:=\coef{\rho}{L_n}=\sum_{k\ge1}
 \sum_{\substack{\gamma_1,\ldots,\gamma_k\in S\\\gamma_1+\cdots+\gamma_k=\rho}}
 Q_n(b_{\gamma_1},\ldots,b_{\gamma_k}),
\end{equation}
a finite sum by Lemma~\ref{meas:lem:neumann}. The factor $1/k!$ compensates for
ordering the distinct coordinate indices, so \eqref{meas:eq:H-finite} handles
repeated exponents and collisions such as $\alpha+\alpha=2\alpha$ correctly.

\subsection{Sufficiency and positivity on all events}

Assume every $b_\gamma\in\ell^2$. Put $H_0=1$ and, for $\rho>0$ in $S^*$,
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:H-limit}
 H_\rho=\sum_{k\ge1}\sum_{\substack{\gamma_1,\ldots,\gamma_k\in S\\\gamma_1+\cdots+\gamma_k=\rho}}
 Q(b_{\gamma_1},\ldots,b_{\gamma_k}),\qquad
 \norm{H_\rho}_2\le\sum_{k\ge1}\sum_{\substack{\gamma_1+\cdots+\gamma_k=\rho\\\gamma_r\in S}}
 \frac1{\sqrt{k!}}\prod_{r=1}^k\norm{b_{\gamma_r}}_2<\infty .
\end{equation}
Set $\mu_\rho=H_\rho P_0$ and assemble the Hahn series of measures.
Lemma~\ref{meas:lem:chaos} gives $\E_0[H_\rho\mid X_1,\ldots,X_n]=H_{\rho,n}$, so the
marginal coefficients are exactly those of $P_n$; each $H_\rho$ with $\rho>0$ has
mean zero, since every nonempty $Z_I$ is centered, so the measure is normalized.
All positive-exponent coefficients are absolutely continuous with respect to
$P_0$, and Corollary~\ref{meas:cor:dominated} gives positivity on \emph{all} Borel
events and identifies the null ideal. This step is essential:
Example~\ref{meas:ex:negative} shows that cylinder positivity alone would not
suffice. Uniqueness is Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:extension}. The bound in
\eqref{meas:eq:H-limit} is checked separately at each exponent; an estimate
summing these bounds over all $\rho$ is neither used nor generally available.

\subsection{Necessity: the least bad exponent cannot be cancelled}

\begin{lemma}[Uniform $L^1$ lower bound; source 15]\label{meas:lem:L1lower}
For real $c_1,\ldots,c_n$ let $T=\sum_jc_jZ_j$, $V=\sum_jc_j^2$, and
$C_\eta=\max\{3,\frac1{\eta(1-\eta)}-3\}$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:L1lower}
 \sqrt{V/C_\eta}\le\E_0\abs T\le\sqrt V .
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The upper bound is Cauchy--Schwarz. A direct calculation gives the fourth-power
expectation $\E_0Z_j^4=\frac1{p_j^0q_j^0}-3\le\frac1{\eta(1-\eta)}-3$. Independence and
centering remove all terms of $\E_0T^4$ except fourth powers and paired indices:
\[
 \E_0T^4=\sum_jc_j^4\,\E_0Z_j^4+6\sum_{i<j}c_i^2c_j^2\le C_\eta V^2 .
\]
H\"older's inequality gives $V=\E_0\abs T^2\le(\E_0\abs T)^{2/3}(\E_0\abs T^4)^{1/3}$;
substituting and rearranging proves the lower bound when $V>0$, and $V=0$ is
immediate.
\end{proof}

Suppose a coefficientwise signed extension exists but some row is not square
summable, and let $\gamma$ be the least such exponent ($S$ is well ordered). At
$\gamma$, \eqref{meas:eq:H-finite} splits as
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:least-bad}
 H_{\gamma,n}=\sum_{j=1}^nb_{j,\gamma}Z_j+R_{\gamma,n}.
\end{equation}
Every exponent in a word of length at least two summing to $\gamma$ is strictly
smaller than $\gamma$; those rows are square summable by minimality, there are
finitely many such words, and Lemma~\ref{meas:lem:chaos} gives
$\sup_n\norm{R_{\gamma,n}}_2<\infty$. On the finite $\sigma$-algebra of the first $n$
coordinates $H_{\gamma,n}$ is the density of the $\gamma$-coefficient signed marginal
relative to $P_0$, so $V_\gamma(n)=\E_0\abs{H_{\gamma,n}}$, which
Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:extension} bounds uniformly. With the $L^2$ bound on
$R_{\gamma,n}$ the first-order sums are bounded in $L^1$ uniformly in $n$, and
Lemma~\ref{meas:lem:L1lower} forces $\sup_n\sum_{j\le n}b_{j,\gamma}^2<\infty$, a
contradiction. This proves Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:interior}.

At a nonminimal exponent, lower-order perturbations contribute many nonlinear
terms; the least-bad-exponent argument shows those already have uniformly
bounded norm. It is this support-controlled induction, not a formal derivative
at $t=0$, that proves necessity for all Hahn exponents at once.

\section{Deterministic coordinates: the exact absolute-summability theorem}
\label{meas:sec:boundary}

Now let $p_j^0=0$ and let each $p_j=\epsilon_j$ in \eqref{meas:eq:perturbation} be
nonnegative; the coefficients $a_{j,\gamma}$ need not all be nonnegative, only the
first nonzero coefficient of each $\epsilon_j$ must be positive. Let
$o=(0,0,\ldots)$ and let $D\subseteq\Omega=\{0,1\}^{\N}$ be the countable set of
configurations with finitely many successes.

\begin{theorem}[Boundary extension criterion; source 15]
\label{meas:thm:boundary}
The finite independent laws with biases $p_j=\epsilon_j\ge0$ admit a
coefficientwise signed extension if and only if
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:boundary-l1}
 a_\gamma=(a_{j,\gamma})_{j\ge1}\in\ell^1\qquad\text{for every }\gamma\in S.
\end{equation}
The extension is then unique and positive; its constant coefficient is
$\delta_o$, all its coefficients are supported on $D$, and its exponent support is
contained in $S^*$.
\end{theorem}

Give $\Omega$ its compact abelian group structure under coordinatewise addition
modulo $2$, let $e_j$ be the configuration with its single success at $j$, and
put $\nu_j=\delta_{e_j}-\delta_o$, so $\norm{\nu_j}_\TV=2$. Finite signed measures form
a Banach algebra under convolution, $\norm{\sigma*\tau}_\TV\le\norm\sigma_\TV\norm\tau_\TV$
(for finite atomic measures by the triangle inequality on products of weights;
in general from the product-measure and pushforward definition). The finite
product law, placed on paths vanishing after $n$, is
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:conv-product}
 \widetilde P_n=\mathop{\ast}_{j=1}^n(\delta_o+\epsilon_j\nu_j),
\end{equation}
each factor being exactly $(1-\epsilon_j)\delta_o+\epsilon_j\delta_{e_j}$. For
$u_1,\ldots,u_k\in\ell^1$ define
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:A-functional}
 A(u_1,\ldots,u_k)=\frac1{k!}\sum_{\substack{j_1,\ldots,j_k\ge1\\\text{pairwise distinct}}}
 \Bigl(\prod_{r=1}^ku_r(j_r)\Bigr)\nu_{j_1}\ast\cdots\ast\nu_{j_k},
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:A-bound}
 \norm{A(u_1,\ldots,u_k)}_\TV\le\frac{2^k}{k!}\prod_{r=1}^k\norm{u_r}_1,
\end{equation}
an ordinary series absolutely convergent in total variation (bound each
convolution norm by $2^k$ and enlarge the distinct-index sum to the product of
$\ell^1$ sums); truncating all indices to $j_r\le n$ converges in total variation.
Assuming \eqref{meas:eq:boundary-l1}, put $\mu_0=\delta_o$ and
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:boundary-coefs}
 \mu_\rho=\sum_{k\ge1}\sum_{\substack{\gamma_1+\cdots+\gamma_k=\rho\\\gamma_r\in S}}
 A(a_{\gamma_1},\ldots,a_{\gamma_k})\qquad(\rho>0),
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:boundary-bound}
 \norm{\mu_\rho}_\TV\le\sum_{k\ge1}\sum_{\substack{\gamma_1+\cdots+\gamma_k=\rho\\\gamma_r\in S}}
 \frac{2^k}{k!}\prod_{r=1}^k\norm{a_{\gamma_r}}_1<\infty ,
\end{equation}
the exponent-word sums being finite. These coefficients are total-variation
limits of the coefficients of \eqref{meas:eq:conv-product}; projecting to the
first $n$ coordinates sends $\nu_j$ to zero for $j>n$, so the limits have the
prescribed marginals. Every nonempty convolution product has total mass zero,
so $\mu$ is normalized, and each term of \eqref{meas:eq:A-functional} lives on
finite-success configurations, a property preserved under total-variation
limits. This proves signed existence with the stated support control.

\subsection{Positivity via exact path weights}

The leading law $\delta_o$ cannot dominate the later coefficients, which put mass
at paths other than $o$; the interior positivity argument does not apply, and an
all-orders atomic argument is needed. Identify a path in $D$ with its finite set
$I\subseteq\N$ of success indices and define
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:path-weight}
 w_I=\Bigl(\prod_{i\in I}\epsilon_i\Bigr)\cprod_{j\notin I}(1-\epsilon_j).
\end{equation}
Here $\cprod$ means: expand each finite product, take the ordinary absolutely
convergent sum of the scalar contributions at each fixed exponent, and assemble
those coefficients into one Hahn series. This exists: at exponent $\rho$ there are
finitely many exponent words, and for each word the scalar sum is bounded by the
product of the corresponding $\ell^1$ row norms divided by $k!$; the common
exponent support lies in $S^*$. The infinite product has constant term $1$ and no
negative exponents, so it is positive, and
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:positive-weights}
 w_I\ge0\qquad(I\text{ finite}).
\end{equation}
Total-variation convergence of the coefficient measures, evaluated on singletons,
identifies the atomic masses:
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:atomic-coefs}
 \mu_\rho(\{I\})=\coef{\rho}{w_I},\qquad
 \sum_{I\text{ finite}}\abs{\coef{\rho}{w_I}}\le\norm{\mu_\rho}_\TV<\infty,
\end{equation}
and consequently, for every Borel $B$,
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:atomic-coef-sum}
 \mu(B)=\csum_{I\in B\cap D}w_I ,
\end{equation}
which is in general \emph{not} a strong Hahn sum. The weights in this
countable family are nonnegative, their supports lie in $S^*$, and
\eqref{meas:eq:atomic-coefs} proves absolute summability at every exponent.
Lemma~\ref{meas:lem:coefpositive} therefore proves positivity on every Borel
set. More precisely, $\mu(B)>0$ exactly when $B\cap D$ contains a path
with nonzero weight; otherwise all coefficient masses of $B$ vanish.

\subsection{Necessity at the least non-absolutely-summable row}

Suppose a coefficientwise signed extension exists and \eqref{meas:eq:boundary-l1}
fails; let $\gamma$ be the least exponent with $a_\gamma\notin\ell^1$. At the first $n$
coordinates its coefficient splits as
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:boundary-first}
 T_{\gamma,n}+R_{\gamma,n},\qquad T_{\gamma,n}=\sum_{j=1}^na_{j,\gamma}(\delta_{e_j}-\delta_o).
\end{equation}
All nonlinear exponent words at $\gamma$ use smaller exponents, so by their absolute
summability, Lemma~\ref{meas:lem:neumann} and \eqref{meas:eq:A-bound} the
remainder has uniformly bounded total variation. The $n+1$ atoms
$o,e_1,\ldots,e_n$ are distinct, so
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:boundary-TV}
 \norm{T_{\gamma,n}}_\TV=\sum_{j=1}^n\abs{a_{j,\gamma}}+\Bigl\lvert\sum_{j=1}^na_{j,\gamma}\Bigr\rvert
 \ge\sum_{j=1}^n\abs{a_{j,\gamma}} .
\end{equation}
The signed extension test bounds $\norm{T_{\gamma,n}+R_{\gamma,n}}_\TV$ uniformly in $n$,
and with the remainder bound this bounds the left side of
\eqref{meas:eq:boundary-TV}, contradicting the choice of $\gamma$. This completes
Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:boundary}.

At an interior law the first-order coefficient is a centered sum of independent
scores, whose $L^1$ norm is comparable to the $\ell^2$ norm of the amplitudes; at a
deterministic law it assigns masses to disjoint single-success paths, whose
variation detects the $\ell^1$ norm directly. Making the scale $t^\gamma$ smaller
cannot repair either divergence: the divergence is an ordinary real number at
one coefficient.

\begin{remark}[The strong threshold at a deterministic baseline; sources 15
and 10]\label{meas:rem:rowfinite}
Under the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:boundary}, the finite Bernoulli laws
admit a strong extension if and only if every row is finitely supported:
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:rowfinite}
 \{j:a_{j,\gamma}\ne0\}\ \text{is finite for every }\gamma\in S.
\end{equation}
This is source~15's corollary ``Finite rows are necessary and sufficient''. With
the common support $S$ it is the special case of
Corollary~\ref{meas:cor:bernoulli}: here $q_j=p_j=\epsilon_j$, the union of the
supports lies in $S$, and $(\epsilon_j)$ is strongly summable exactly when every row
is finite. A signed strong extension is coefficientwise
(Corollary~\ref{meas:cor:two-axioms}) and hence, by the uniqueness in
Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:boundary}, is the positive one. Source~15's direct proof:
finite rows are in $\ell^1$, and at a fixed output exponent finitely many exponent
words use finitely many coordinates, so each $\mu_\rho$ in
\eqref{meas:eq:boundary-coefs} is finitely supported and
Corollary~\ref{meas:cor:two-axioms} applies; conversely, at the least exponent
$\gamma$ with an infinitely supported row, $\mu_\gamma=\sum_ja_{j,\gamma}(\delta_{e_j}-\delta_o)+R_\gamma$
with $R_\gamma$ finitely supported (its rows are smaller and finite), so $\mu_\gamma$ has
infinitely many atoms, contradicting Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:atomic}. For instance
$p_j=t^{j\alpha}$ gives an infinite strong probability with infinitesimal
coordinate success probabilities at any rank, while $p_j=c_jt^\alpha$ with
infinitely many $c_j>0$ is never strong, even when $\sum_jc_j<\infty$ and a
coefficientwise extension exists. So three requirements occur in one problem:
finite support per exponent row (strong addition), $\ell^1$ per row (perturbing a
point mass), and $\ell^2$ per row (perturbing a nondegenerate product law).
\end{remark}

\section{A mixed interior--boundary classification}\label{meas:sec:mixed}

A tensor product of signed corrections is not positive coefficient by
coefficient, so combining the two theorems needs care.

\begin{theorem}[Mixed extension criterion; source 15]\label{meas:thm:mixed}
Let $p_j=p_j^0+\sum_{\gamma\in S}a_{j,\gamma}t^\gamma$ with $p_j^0\in\{0,1\}\cup[\eta,1-\eta]$,
$0<\eta\le\frac12$, a common well-ordered $S\subseteq\Gamma_{>0}$, and $0\le p_j\le1$
in $\KR$. Put $J_{\mathrm{int}}=\{j:0<p_j^0<1\}$ and
$J_{\mathrm{bd}}=\{j:p_j^0\in\{0,1\}\}$. The finite independent laws admit a
coefficientwise signed extension if and only if, for each $\gamma\in S$,
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:mixed}
 \sum_{j\in J_{\mathrm{int}}}\frac{a_{j,\gamma}^2}{p_j^0(1-p_j^0)}<\infty
 \qquad\text{and}\qquad
 \sum_{j\in J_{\mathrm{bd}}}\abs{a_{j,\gamma}}<\infty .
\end{equation}
The extension is then unique and positive, with exponent support in $S^*$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Flip every coordinate with $p_j^0=1$, replacing $X_j$ by $1-X_j$: its baseline
becomes $0$ and its success bias $1-p_j\ge0$; the row changes sign and keeps its
$\ell^1$ norm. So assume all boundary baselines are $0$. Necessity follows by
marginalizing to the two coordinate subsequences: a pushforward of a finite
coefficient measure is finite and the common support is preserved, so
Theorems~\ref{meas:thm:interior} and~\ref{meas:thm:boundary} apply (finite or empty
subsequences satisfy the conditions automatically).

For sufficiency let $\tau=\sum_\alpha\tau_\alpha t^\alpha$ be the interior extension and
$\beta=\sum_\delta\beta_\delta t^\delta$ the boundary extension, both with exponents in
$S^*$, and define on the product of their sample spaces
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:mixed-tensor}
 \mu_\rho=\sum_{\substack{\alpha,\delta\in S^*\\\alpha+\delta=\rho}}\tau_\alpha\otimes\beta_\delta ,
 \qquad
 \norm{\mu_\rho}_\TV\le\sum_{\alpha+\delta=\rho}\norm{\tau_\alpha}_\TV\norm{\beta_\delta}_\TV<\infty,
\end{equation}
a finite sum by Lemma~\ref{meas:lem:neumann}. Its cylinder values are the
products of the prescribed marginal weights; normalization and uniqueness
follow. For positivity: $\beta$ has nonnegative weights $w_I$ on the countable set
of finite-success boundary paths. For a Borel event $A$ of the product let $A_I$
be its interior section at $I$; then
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:mixed-sections}
 \mu(A)=\csum_Iw_I\,\tau(A_I),
\end{equation}
absolutely convergent at every exponent (use the finite sum over
$\alpha+\delta=\rho$, $\abs{\tau_\alpha(A_I)}\le\norm{\tau_\alpha}_\TV$ and
$\sum_I\abs{\coef\delta{w_I}}\le\norm{\beta_\delta}_\TV$). Each summand
$w_I\tau(A_I)$ is nonnegative with exponents in $S^*$, and the
coefficientwise positivity lemma (Lemma~\ref{meas:lem:coefpositive}) gives
$\mu(A)\ge0$. Flipping back preserves positivity.
\end{proof}

\section{Consequences, sharp examples, and the boundary of the theorems}
\label{meas:sec:coef-examples}

\subsection{Rigidity of a constant surreal bias}

\begin{corollary}[No coefficientwise nonreal i.i.d.\ bias; source 15]
\label{meas:cor:iid}
Let $p\in\KR$ with $0\le p\le1$. The consistent Bernoulli laws
$P_n(x)=p^{x_1+\cdots+x_n}(1-p)^{n-(x_1+\cdots+x_n)}$ have a coefficientwise
signed extension to $\{0,1\}^{\N}$ if and only if $p$ is an ordinary real
number. The extension is then the usual real Bernoulli probability, embedded as
the exponent-zero coefficient.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Write $p=q+\epsilon$ with $q\in[0,1]\cap\R$ its constant coefficient and $\epsilon$ of
positive support. If $\epsilon=0$ the classical product measure is the extension.
Otherwise let $\gamma=v(\epsilon)$ and $a\ne0$ its coefficient. If $0<q<1$, the row at
$\gamma$ is the constant nonzero row $a/\sqrt{q(1-q)}$, not in $\ell^2$, and
Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:interior} (with $\eta=\min(q,1-q)$) excludes an extension.
If $q=0$, positivity of $p$ permits Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:boundary}, and the
constant row $a$ is not in $\ell^1$. If $q=1$, exchange success and failure.
\end{proof}

For $p=t^\alpha$ the obstruction is visible at the first infinitesimal
coefficient: the first-$n$ law has $\alpha$-coefficient $-n$ at $o$ and $+1$ at each
of $e_1,\ldots,e_n$, of total variation exactly $2n$, and no finite signed measure
has all these marginals. For $p=q+at^\alpha$ with $0<q<1$ the first coefficient
variation is
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:binomial-variation}
 V_\alpha(n)=\frac{\abs a}{q(1-q)}\,\E\bigl\lvert\operatorname{Bin}(n,q)-nq\bigr\rvert ,
\end{equation}
which Lemma~\ref{meas:lem:L1lower} bounds above and below by positive constant
multiples of $\sqrt n$; no central limit theorem is needed.

The corollary holds for surreal biases through
Convention~\ref{meas:conv:surreal}. More generally, a purported coefficientwise
normal-form measure with values in $\No$ and a common support can be placed in a
set-sized Hahn workspace by taking the additive subgroup generated by that
support, so enlarging the exponent workspace cannot evade this obstruction.
The i.i.d.\ law with real bias $p\in(0,1)$ exists coefficientwise but not strongly
(Section~\ref{meas:sub:traps}, Trap 2).

\subsection{Examples distinguishing the thresholds}

Fix $\alpha>0$. The following conclusions are exact.
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{0.27\linewidth}p{0.66\linewidth}@{}}
\toprule
Coordinate biases & Infinite extension conclusion\\
\midrule
$p_j=\frac12+t^\alpha/j$ & A coefficientwise Hahn probability exists, since
$\sum j^{-2}<\infty$. It is not strong: its constant coefficient is an atomless
Bernoulli product, contrary to Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:atomic}.\\[3pt]
$p_j=\frac12+t^\alpha/\sqrt j$ & No coefficientwise signed extension, since
$\sum j^{-1}=\infty$.\\[3pt]
$p_j=t^\alpha/j^2$ & A coefficientwise Hahn probability exists; it is not strong:
at the single-success path $e_j$ the coefficient of $t^\alpha$ is $j^{-2}$, so
countably many disjoint singletons contribute at one exponent.\\[3pt]
$p_j=t^\alpha/j$ & No coefficientwise signed extension.\\[3pt]
$p_j=t^{j\alpha}$ & A strong Hahn probability exists
(Remark~\ref{meas:rem:rowfinite}).\\[3pt]
$p_j=p\in\KR\setminus\R$ & No coefficientwise signed i.i.d.\ extension.\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\paragraph{Genuine higher-rank examples.} Take $\Gamma=\Z\alpha\oplus\Z\beta$ ordered
so that $0<n\alpha<\beta$ for every integer $n\ge1$ (a surreal realization is
$\alpha=1$, $\beta=\omega$), and $p_j=\frac12+t^\alpha/j+t^\beta/j^2$. Both rows satisfy the
interior criterion, so an extension exists. At exponent $\beta$ only the length-one
word $(\beta)$ contributes, since no finite multiple of $\alpha$ equals $\beta$; at
$\beta+2\alpha$ the three orderings of $(\beta,\alpha,\alpha)$ contribute, with the factorial
normalization of \eqref{meas:eq:Q}. There is no limiting passage from $n\alpha$ to
$\beta$. More generally one can prescribe arbitrarily large finite values of
$\norm{b_\gamma}_2$ at successive exponents, or use an uncountable well-ordered $S$
in a suitable group; Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:interior} applies row by row, and no
common real-analytic perturbation parameter is required.

\subsection{Why uniform separation from \texorpdfstring{$0$}{0} and
\texorpdfstring{$1$}{1} matters}

The weighted $\ell^2$ condition is sufficient at an interior baseline even without
uniform separation (the construction uses only independence and variance one),
but its necessity used the uniform bound on $\E_0Z_j^4$.

\begin{proposition}[A universal $\ell^1$ sufficient condition; source 15]
\label{meas:prop:general-l1}
Let $0<p_j^0<1$ be arbitrary, with no uniform separation from the endpoints, and
suppose every row $a_\gamma$ in \eqref{meas:eq:perturbation} is in $\ell^1$. Then the
finite Bernoulli laws admit a unique coefficientwise Hahn probability extension
whose coefficients are absolutely continuous with respect to $P_0$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
For each exponent define the centered score
$h_{j,\gamma}=a_{j,\gamma}(X_j-p_j^0)/(p_j^0(1-p_j^0))$; a two-point calculation gives
$\E_0\abs{h_{j,\gamma}}=2\abs{a_{j,\gamma}}$. For distinct coordinates, independence makes
the $L^1$ norm of a product of scores the product of their $L^1$ norms, so for each
exponent word the distinct-coordinate expansion converges absolutely in $L^1$,
with bound $\frac{2^k}{k!}\prod_r\norm{a_{\gamma_r}}_1$. Finitely many words occur at
each output exponent; their sums are $L^1(P_0)$ densities with the prescribed
cylinder coefficients and mean zero at positive exponents. Absolute continuity
gives positivity as in Corollary~\ref{meas:cor:dominated}, and scalar uniqueness
gives uniqueness.
\end{proof}

\begin{example}[Failure of unrestricted weighted-$\ell^2$ necessity; source 15]
\label{meas:ex:near-boundary}
Let $p_j^0=2^{-4j}$ and $\epsilon_j=2^{-j}t^\alpha$. The row is in $\ell^1$, so
Proposition~\ref{meas:prop:general-l1} gives an extension, but
$\sum_j2^{-2j}/(2^{-4j}(1-2^{-4j}))=\sum_j2^{2j}/(1-2^{-4j})=+\infty$. The
equivalence of Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:interior} is false without its uniform
interior hypothesis.
\end{example}

\subsection{No ordinary continuity in the field order}

For disjoint events each coefficient obeys ordinary countable additivity. It does
not follow that the partial sums of their Hahn-valued probabilities converge in
an order or valuation topology, nor that an ordinary decreasing limit of real
leading coefficients eventually falls below a positive infinitesimal tolerance;
these are different statements with different quantifiers. The proofs of this
part use only finite total variation at each coefficient and support-controlled
assembly, and they are not identified with convergence in the surreal fine
topology or with an ultrafilter sum.

\section{Complex coefficients, conditioning, and observables}
\label{meas:sec:coef-complex}

\begin{proposition}[Surcomplex analogue; source 15]\label{meas:prop:complex}
Let $a_{j,\gamma}\in\C$, take the values of the finite Bernoulli-form weights in
$\KC$, and keep real baseline probabilities $p_j^0$. For $p_j^0\in[\eta,1-\eta]$, a
coefficientwise complex extension exists if and only if each row
$\bigl(a_{j,\gamma}/\sqrt{p_j^0(1-p_j^0)}\bigr)_j$ is in complex $\ell^2$; at the baseline
$p_j^0=0$ the exact condition is $a_\gamma\in\ell^1(\C)$. These extensions are unique
and have the support control and norm bounds of the real theorems. No positivity
assertion is made for complex weights.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Orthogonality \eqref{meas:eq:orthogonality} gives the same $L^2$ estimate with
squared moduli, so interior sufficiency is unchanged. For necessity, if
$T=\sum_jc_jZ_j$ with complex $c_j$, its real or imaginary part has variance at
least $\frac12\sum_j\abs{c_j}^2$, and Lemma~\ref{meas:lem:L1lower} for that part gives
$\E_0\abs T\ge(2C_\eta)^{-1/2}(\sum_j\abs{c_j}^2)^{1/2}$; the least-bad-exponent
argument is unchanged. At the endpoint the convolution series converges
absolutely in the Banach space of finite complex measures with the same bound,
and the first-order total variation is $\sum_j\abs{a_{j,\gamma}}+\abs{\sum_ja_{j,\gamma}}$.
Uniqueness is the complex case of Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:extension}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Conditioning on infinitesimal events; source 15]
\label{meas:prop:conditioning}
Let $\mu$ be a coefficientwise Hahn probability and $B$ an event with $\mu(B)>0$.
Then $\mu(A\mid B)=\mu(A\cap B)/\mu(B)$ is again a coefficientwise Hahn probability
on the same space.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Each $A\mapsto\mu_\gamma(A\cap B)$ is a finite signed measure with the original common
support, and $c=\mu(B)^{-1}$ is a Hahn series. The sum of two well-ordered
supports is well ordered with finitely many contributing pairs per exponent, so
every coefficient of $c\,\mu(\,\cdot\cap B)$ is a finite linear combination of finite
signed measures, with a common well-ordered support. Positivity and the bound $1$
follow from $0\le\mu(A\cap B)\le\mu(B)$; normalization is immediate; and any apparent
negative-exponent coefficient must vanish, since the values lie in $[0,1]_{\KR}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{example}[The location of one rare success; source 15]
\label{meas:ex:conditioning}
Let $p_j=c_jt^\alpha$ with $c_j>0$ and $C=\sum_jc_j<\infty$. The endpoint theorem gives a
coefficientwise probability on finite-success paths. For the event $B$ of at least
one success,
\[
 \mu(B)=1-\cprod_j(1-c_jt^\alpha)=Ct^\alpha+\text{terms of larger exponent},
\]
so conditioning on $B$ is legitimate, and
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:rare-location}
 \st\mu(\,\cdot\mid B)=\sum_{j=1}^\infty\frac{c_j}C\,\delta_{e_j}.
\end{equation}
Indeed the $\alpha$-coefficient of $\mu(\,\cdot\cap B)$ is exactly $\sum_jc_j\delta_{e_j}$,
since paths with two or more successes first occur at exponent at least $2\alpha$,
and multiplication by $\mu(B)^{-1}$ gives \eqref{meas:eq:rare-location} at exponent
zero. The standard part is an ordinary countably additive probability with
infinitely many atoms, not a strong sum of constant series; this is Trap~2 of
Section~\ref{meas:sub:traps}.
\end{example}

\paragraph{Bounded ordinary observables.} For a bounded ordinary complex Borel
function $f$ and a coefficientwise Hahn measure $\mu$ define
\begin{equation}\label{meas:eq:coef-integral}
 \int_Xf\,d\mu=\sum_{\gamma\in W}\Bigl(\int_Xf\,d\mu_\gamma\Bigr)t^\gamma\in\KC ,
\end{equation}
well defined because its support lies in $W$ and
$\abs{\int f\,d\mu_\gamma}\le\norm f_\infty\norm{\mu_\gamma}_\TV$. Finite products of
coordinate indicators have the probabilities \eqref{meas:eq:finite-intro}, by the
marginal identities already proved. If a uniformly bounded sequence of ordinary
observables converges pointwise, dominated convergence applies separately to
every coefficient measure; the conclusion is coefficientwise convergence of the
integrals, not convergence in the fine surreal topology. Integrating arbitrary
Hahn-valued functions would need further joint support and integrability
conditions and is not asserted by source~15; for countably many exponents
source~16 supplies such conditions and an integral
(Section~\ref{meas:cw:sec:integral}). (For strong measures the integral
\eqref{meas:eq:integral} is defined for every scalar function, bounded or not.)

\section{Countably many scales: one real control measure}
\label{meas:cw:sec:control}

Sections~\ref{meas:cw:sec:control}--\ref{meas:cw:sec:boundary} are source~16's.
Source~16 read this report at a later repository snapshot and took the
coefficientwise class of Definition~\ref{meas:def:coefficientwise} as its
starting point. It restricts to \emph{countably many} exponents, and under that
restriction develops a real control measure, decompositions, an integral of
Hahn-valued functions with a Radon--Nikodym theorem, products, and conditional
expectation. The strong/coefficientwise distinction and the null-ideal
criterion are this report's (sources~15 and~18), and source~16 attributes them
so. Several of its steps are, in its own words, reformulations of standard
scalar measure theory; the scalar Radon--Nikodym, Lebesgue decomposition,
Fubini, conditional-expectation and Carath\'eodory theorems it applies are
classical inputs~\cite{Bogachev}. Its restrictions (countably many scales,
finite real coefficient variation) are substantive, and each proof below says
where it uses them.

\begin{convention}[Countable scales; notation of
Sections~\ref{meas:cw:sec:control}--\ref{meas:cw:sec:boundary}]
\label{meas:cw:conv:notation}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item $\Kc\subseteq\KR$ is the set of real Hahn series with \emph{countable}
support. It is a subfield: sums and products preserve countable support
(Lemma~\ref{meas:lem:sumalgebra}), and after a leading monomial is extracted,
the inverse $(1-\epsilon)^{-1}=\sum_k\epsilon^k$ of an $\epsilon$ with positive
countable support has support in the monoid of finite words over that support,
which is countable, with finitely many words at each exponent
(Lemma~\ref{meas:lem:neumann}). When every well-ordered subset of $\Gamma$ is
countable, as for $\Gamma=\Z,\Q,\R$, one has $\Kc=\KR$. Surreal values are
obtained through Convention~\ref{meas:conv:surreal}; coefficientwise summation is
transported along that specified embedding and is not identified with any
convergence in $\No$.
\item A \emph{countable-scale measure} is a real coefficientwise Hahn measure
(Definition~\ref{meas:def:coefficientwise}, $k=\R$) whose exponent set $W$ is
countable. Its values lie in $\Kc$. Positivity and probability are those of
Definition~\ref{meas:def:positive}.
\item \emph{Renamed from source~16.} Its density $m$ is written $\theta$ here
($m_k$ are power moments, Convention~\ref{meas:conv:terms}(a), and $m$ is Haar
measure in Remark~\ref{meas:rem:dynamics}). Its ``coherent Hahn function'' is a
\emph{common-support Hahn function} here, because ``coherent'' already means
consistent cylinder data. Its integrability domain $\mathcal D(\mu)$ is written
$\Lad(\mu)$. Its order variation $\abs\sigma$ is written $\abs\sigma_{\mathrm{ord}}$,
because $\abs\nu$ is the total-variation measure of a scalar $\nu$ throughout. Its
$\varepsilon$, which is $t$ with $\Gamma\supseteq\Z$, is written $t$ (the
$\epsilon_j$ of Sections~\ref{meas:sec:interior}--\ref{meas:sec:mixed} are
Bernoulli perturbations). Its $\int^{\mathrm{cw}}$ and $\to_{\mathrm{cw}}$ are
written $\intc$ and $\tocoef$, matching $\csum$.
\item $\lambda$ is a finite positive real \emph{control} measure, as
$\lambda_{<\gamma}$ in \eqref{meas:eq:control}; the cylinder weights
$\lambda_n(x)$ of Section~\ref{meas:sec:coef-extension} are unrelated.
$\mathcal G\subseteq\mathcal G'$ denote sub-$\sigma$-algebras.
\item The Hahn and Hahn--Jordan \emph{decompositions} of
Section~\ref{meas:cw:sec:jordan} are named after Hans Hahn's theorem on signed
measures; Hahn \emph{series} carry the same name. A Hahn-valued function $f$ is
\emph{$\ge0$ almost everywhere} if $f(x)\ge0$ in $\KR$ outside a null set.
\end{enumerate}
\end{convention}

For series $a_n,a$ whose supports lie in one fixed countable well-ordered set
$W$, write $a_n\tocoef a$ when every real coefficient converges. This is the
product topology on the coefficient space $\R^W$. The common $W$ is part of the
hypothesis; without it, limits of coefficients need not define a Hahn series.
Multiplication by a fixed scalar is continuous between the relevant
support-controlled spaces, since each coefficient of a product is a finite sum
(Lemma~\ref{meas:lem:sumalgebra}).

\subsection{Elementary properties}

A countable-scale measure is its coefficient family, not a particular
enumeration of $W$; zero coefficients may be inserted or removed, and equality
of the $\Kc$-valued set functions determines every coefficient measure. For
disjoint events \eqref{meas:eq:coef-add} holds with ordinary real total
variation on the right. Restrictions, measurable pushforwards, finite linear
combinations and multiples by a scalar $c\in\Kc$ are again countable-scale
measures; for $c\mu$ each new coefficient is a finite linear combination of old
ones, and the new support lies in the sumset $\supp c+W$. For positive $\mu$,
finite additivity gives monotonicity, finite subadditivity and
$\mu(A)\le\mu(X)$. For $A_n\uparrow A$ or $A_n\downarrow A$, continuity of the
finite signed coefficient measures gives $\mu(A_n)\tocoef\mu(A)$. This is
continuity of a specified measure, not an order-completeness principle.

\subsection{A control measure and the Hahn density}

Enumerate $W=\{\gamma_1,\gamma_2,\ldots\}$ (finitely, if $W$ is finite; the
enumeration need not follow the order of $W$) and put
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:control}
 \lambda=\sum_{k\ge1}\frac{2^{-k}}{1+\norm{\mu_{\gamma_k}}_\TV}\,\abs{\mu_{\gamma_k}} .
\end{equation}
Then $\lambda(X)\le1$ and
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:control-null}
 \lambda(A)=0\iff\abs{\mu_\gamma}(A)=0\ \text{for every }\gamma\in W .
\end{equation}
This is the control \eqref{meas:eq:control} of Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:nullideal}
formed over all of $W$ instead of over the predecessors of one exponent. More
generally, any finite positive measure $\lambda$ with $\abs{\mu_\gamma}\ll\lambda$
for every $\gamma$ is called a \emph{control} of $\mu$. By the scalar
Radon--Nikodym theorem $\mu_\gamma=\theta_\gamma\lambda$ with
$\theta_\gamma\in L^1(\lambda)$, and because $W$ is countable the representatives
can be chosen simultaneously outside one null set. Thus
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:density}
 \theta(x)=\sum_{\gamma\in W}\theta_\gamma(x)\,t^\gamma,\qquad
 \mu(A)=\intc_A\theta\,d\lambda
 :=\sum_{\gamma\in W}\Bigl(\int_A\theta_\gamma\,d\lambda\Bigr)t^\gamma .
\end{equation}
$\theta$ is the \emph{Hahn density} of $\mu$ relative to $\lambda$.

\begin{lemma}[Integrating a Hahn sign; source 16]\label{meas:cw:lem:sign}
Let $W$ be countable and well ordered, $\lambda$ a finite positive measure, and
$h=\sum_{\gamma\in W}h_\gamma t^\gamma$ with every $h_\gamma\in L^1(\lambda)$.
The following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item $h(x)\ge0$ for $\lambda$-almost every $x$;
\item $\intc_Ah\,d\lambda\ge0$ for every measurable $A$.
\end{enumerate}
Under these conditions $\intc_Ah\,d\lambda=0$ if and only if $h=0$ almost
everywhere on $A$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Suppose $h\ge0$ outside a null set $N_0$. The sets
\[
 E_\gamma=\{x:h_\delta(x)=0\text{ for every }\delta<\gamma,\ h_\gamma(x)>0\}
 \qquad(\gamma\in W)
\]
are measurable, since only countably many predecessor conditions occur, and
outside $N_0$ they partition $\{h>0\}$ according to the leading exponent. If $h$
is not zero almost everywhere on $A$, some $A\cap E_\gamma$ has positive measure,
because $W$ is countable; choose the least such $\gamma$. The union of the sets
$A\cap E_\delta$, $\delta<\gamma$, is null, again by countability. Hence every
coefficient $h_\delta$ with $\delta<\gamma$ vanishes almost everywhere on $A$, while
$h_\gamma\ge0$ almost everywhere on $A$ and $h_\gamma>0$ on $A\cap E_\gamma$. So the
coefficients of $\intc_Ah\,d\lambda$ before $\gamma$ vanish and the one at $\gamma$
is positive. This proves (ii) and the assertion about zero.

Conversely, if $h<0$ on a set of positive measure, one of the measurable sets
$F_\gamma=\{x:h_\delta(x)=0\text{ for }\delta<\gamma,\ h_\gamma(x)<0\}$ has positive
measure. On $F_\gamma$ all earlier coefficient integrals vanish and the
$\gamma$-coefficient integral is negative, contradicting (ii).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Scalar-control representation and positivity; source 16]
\label{meas:cw:thm:representation}
Every countable-scale measure $\mu$ has the representation
\eqref{meas:cw:eq:density} relative to every control, in particular relative
to \eqref{meas:cw:eq:control}. It is positive if and only if $\theta\ge0$
almost everywhere. If $\mu$ is positive and $\lambda$ is
\eqref{meas:cw:eq:control}, then $\theta>0$ almost everywhere and
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:null-equivalence}
 \mu(A)=0\iff\lambda(A)=0 .
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The positivity criterion is Lemma~\ref{meas:cw:lem:sign} with $h=\theta$. For
\eqref{meas:cw:eq:control}, the set on which every $\theta_\gamma$ vanishes is null
for every $\abs{\mu_\gamma}=\abs{\theta_\gamma}\lambda$, hence for $\lambda$; so a
positive $\mu$ has $\theta>0$ almost everywhere, and the zero clause of the sign
lemma gives \eqref{meas:cw:eq:null-equivalence}.
\end{proof}

The criterion allows negative coefficient measures: $(1-t)\lambda$ is
positive, its first density coefficient being positive. By contrast
$\lambda-t\delta_a$ is negative on $\{a\}$ when $\lambda$ is atomless.

\subsection{The density criterion and the null-ideal criterion}
\label{meas:cw:sub:nullideal}

Source~16 calls Theorem~\ref{meas:cw:thm:representation} the countable-scale
density form of this report's null-ideal criterion. The precise relation is
the following; the proposition is added in the merge.

\begin{proposition}[Pointwise form of the null-ideal criterion]
\label{meas:cw:prop:nullideal}
Let $\mu$ be a countable-scale measure with exponent set $W$, let $\lambda$ be any
control of $\mu$, and let $\theta$ be the Hahn density. For $\gamma\in W$ put
$Z_\gamma=\{x:\theta_\delta(x)=0\text{ for every }\delta\in W,\ \delta<\gamma\}$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item A measurable $A$ lies in the $\sigma$-ideal $\mathcal J_{<\gamma}$ of
\eqref{meas:eq:nullideal} if and only if $\lambda(A\setminus Z_\gamma)=0$. In
particular $Z_\gamma\in\mathcal J_{<\gamma}$ is its largest member up to
$\lambda$-null sets.
\item Condition (c) of Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:nullideal} at $\gamma$, namely
$\mu_\gamma^-(A)=0$ for $A\in\mathcal J_{<\gamma}$, holds if and only if
$\theta_\gamma\ge0$ almost everywhere on $Z_\gamma$.
\item Conditions (a)--(c) of Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:nullideal} are equivalent to
$\theta\ge0$ $\lambda$-almost everywhere.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(i) $\abs{\mu_\delta}(A)=\int_A\abs{\theta_\delta}\,d\lambda$, so $A\in\mathcal
J_{<\gamma}$ exactly when every $\theta_\delta$, $\delta<\gamma$, vanishes almost
everywhere on $A$; these are countably many conditions. (ii)
$\mu_\gamma^-(A)=\int_A\theta_\gamma^-\,d\lambda$. If $\theta_\gamma^-=0$ almost
everywhere on $Z_\gamma$, then by (i) $\mu_\gamma^-(A)=0$ for $A\in\mathcal
J_{<\gamma}$; conversely $\mu_\gamma^-(Z_\gamma)=0$ forces $\theta_\gamma^-=0$ almost
everywhere on $Z_\gamma$. (iii) At a point $x$, $\theta(x)\ge0$ holds if and only
if $\theta_\gamma(x)\ge0$ for every $\gamma$ with $x\in Z_\gamma$: if $\theta(x)\ne0$
has leading exponent $\gamma_0$, then $x\in Z_\gamma$ exactly for
$\gamma\le\gamma_0$, and $\theta_\gamma(x)=0$ for $\gamma<\gamma_0$. Since $W$ is countable,
``for almost every $x$ and every $\gamma$'' is the same as ``for every $\gamma$ and
almost every $x$''. With (ii), $\theta\ge0$ almost everywhere is therefore
equivalent to (c) at every $\gamma$, and Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:nullideal} gives
the equivalence with (a) and (b). (Alternatively, Lemma~\ref{meas:cw:lem:sign}
with $h=\theta$ gives $\theta\ge0$ almost everywhere $\Leftrightarrow$ (a) directly.)
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Two routes, and what each gives]\label{meas:cw:rem:routes}
For countable $W$ the positivity clause of
Theorem~\ref{meas:cw:thm:representation} therefore has two proofs: source~16's,
integrating the leading-sign strata (Lemma~\ref{meas:cw:lem:sign}), and the
route through Proposition~\ref{meas:cw:prop:nullideal} and
Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:nullideal} (sources~15 and~18), whose transfinite
induction uses the full null ideals and no control. Read the other way, the
sign lemma is a second proof of (a)$\Leftrightarrow$(c) of
Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:nullideal} for countable $W$. Neither statement contains
the other. Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:nullideal} holds for well-ordered $W$ of every
cardinality, where no single control need exist
(\texttt{herg:ex:uncountable}, Remark~\ref{meas:rem:nullideal}, and
Example~\ref{meas:cw:ex:omega1}). The density form gives what the null-ideal
theorem does not state: the null ideal of a positive countable-scale measure is
the null ideal of one finite real measure \eqref{meas:cw:eq:null-equivalence},
and positivity becomes a pointwise inequality between Hahn series, on which the
integration theory of Sections~\ref{meas:cw:sec:integral}--\ref{meas:cw:sec:probability}
is built. The condition $\mu_\gamma^-\ll\lambda_{<\gamma}$ of
Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:nullideal} is the coefficient-by-coefficient form; $\theta\ge0$
almost everywhere is the pointwise form. The Lean formalization recorded in
\path{docs/FORMALIZATION.md} covers the null-ideal theorem, its control-measure
form and Corollary~\ref{meas:cor:dominated}; it does not cover the density form
or anything else in Sections~\ref{meas:cw:sec:control}--\ref{meas:cw:sec:boundary}.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Corollary~\ref{meas:cor:dominated} in density form]
\label{meas:cw:rem:dominated}
With $W$ countable, Corollary~\ref{meas:cor:dominated} is the case of
Theorem~\ref{meas:cw:thm:representation} in which the leading law $P_0$ is itself
a control: relative to $\lambda=P_0$ the density is
$\theta=1+\sum_{\gamma>0}\theta_\gamma t^\gamma$, whose leading coefficient is the
constant $1$, so $\theta>0$ everywhere and $\mu(A)=0$ exactly when $P_0(A)=0$.
Source~16's construction principle \eqref{meas:cw:eq:unit-density} is the same
mechanism. The density criterion goes further in two directions: the leading
exponent of $\theta(x)$ may vary with $x$, only $\theta>0$ almost everywhere being
required, and a later coefficient need not be absolutely continuous
with respect to $P_0$, provided it is nonnegative where every earlier density
vanishes; the law $\lambda+t\delta_0+t^2\delta_1$ of
Example~\ref{meas:cw:ex:worked} is positive although $\delta_0\not\ll\lambda$.
Corollary~\ref{meas:cor:dominated} itself needs no countability, and for
uncountable $W$ it is not a case of the density theorem. For the interior
extension of Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:interior} with $S$ countable, the Hahn
density relative to $P_0$ is $\sum_{\rho\in S^*}H_\rho t^\rho$ with $H_0=1$, by
\eqref{meas:eq:density-measure}.
\end{remark}

\begin{example}[Example~\ref{meas:ex:negative} in density form]
\label{meas:cw:ex:negative-density}
For $\mu=P_0+t^\alpha(\delta_y-\delta_x)$ take the control
$\lambda=P_0+\delta_x+\delta_y$. Then $\theta=1$ at $P_0$-almost every point,
$\theta(y)=t^\alpha$ and $\theta(x)=-t^\alpha$. The density is negative exactly on
$\{x\}$, a set of positive $\lambda$-mass, and $\mu(\{x\})=-t^\alpha$; the
cylinder masses are positive because every cylinder also carries $P_0$-mass.
\end{example}

\section{Null sets, the Hahn--Jordan decomposition, and constructions}
\label{meas:cw:sec:jordan}

\subsection{Completion and the countable chain condition}

\begin{corollary}[Completion; source 16]\label{meas:cw:cor:completion}
The null sets of a positive countable-scale measure form a $\sigma$-ideal. The
measure extends uniquely to the completion of $\Sigma$ obtained by adjoining all
subsets of null measurable sets, and this completion is the ordinary completion
for any finite real control with the same null sets.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Use \eqref{meas:cw:eq:null-equivalence}. Each real coefficient, being
absolutely continuous with respect to $\lambda$, extends uniquely to the scalar
completion, and the Hahn assembly of the extensions is positive by the same
density.
\end{proof}

A family of pairwise disjoint measurable sets of positive $\mu$-mass is
countable, since each member has positive $\lambda$-mass and $\lambda$ is finite. So
on an uncountable space with measurable singletons a countable-scale
probability cannot give positive mass to every singleton. This does not
contradict Remark~\ref{meas:rem:many-atoms}: its strong probabilities with
uncountably many atoms have uncountable exponent support and are outside the
countable-scale class. Nor does it rule out such assignments under a different
axiom (Section~\ref{meas:cw:sub:numerosity}).

\subsection{A genuine Hahn decomposition}

\begin{theorem}[Hahn--Jordan decomposition; source 16]\label{meas:cw:thm:jordan}
Every countable-scale measure $\sigma$ has a measurable decomposition
$X=P\sqcup N$ such that every measurable subset of $P$ has nonnegative
$\sigma$-mass and every measurable subset of $N$ has nonpositive $\sigma$-mass.
There are unique positive countable-scale measures $\sigma^+,\sigma^-$,
concentrated on disjoint measurable sets, with $\sigma=\sigma^+-\sigma^-$, namely
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:jordan}
 \sigma^+(A)=\sigma(A\cap P),\qquad\sigma^-(A)=-\sigma(A\cap N).
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Choose a control and write $\sigma=\intc s\,d\lambda$. The sign sets
$P=\{s\ge0\}$ and $N=\{s<0\}$ are measurable because $s$ has countable common
support. Apply Lemma~\ref{meas:cw:lem:sign} to $s\ind_P$ and $-s\ind_N$;
multiplying coefficients by an indicator preserves integrability and support,
so \eqref{meas:cw:eq:jordan} defines countable-scale measures. For uniqueness let
$\sigma=\alpha-\beta$ with positive $\alpha,\beta$ concentrated on disjoint $U,V$. On
$U$ the restriction of $\sigma$ is $\alpha\ge0$, on $V$ it is $-\beta\le0$, and on
the complement of $U\cup V$ it vanishes. A set that is positive for one
decomposition and negative for another carries the zero restriction, since
every measurable subset has mass both $\ge0$ and $\le0$. Comparing $P,N$ with
$U,V$ piece by piece gives $\alpha=\sigma^+$ and $\beta=\sigma^-$ on every set.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Order variation is attained; source 16]\label{meas:cw:thm:variation}
Put $\abs\sigma_{\mathrm{ord}}=\sigma^++\sigma^-$. For every measurable $A$,
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:variation}
 \abs\sigma_{\mathrm{ord}}(A)=\max_{\mathcal P}\sum_{B\in\mathcal P}\abs{\sigma(B)},
\end{equation}
the maximum, in $\Kc$, over finite measurable partitions $\mathcal P$ of $A$; the
two-piece partition $\{A\cap P,A\cap N\}$ attains it.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
For every $B$,
$\abs{\sigma(B)}=\abs{\sigma^+(B)-\sigma^-(B)}\le\sigma^+(B)+\sigma^-(B)$; sum over a
partition. The sign partition gives equality by \eqref{meas:cw:eq:jordan}.
\end{proof}

Here an order supremum causes no difficulty (compare
Section~\ref{meas:cw:sub:orderlimits}): it is a maximum, proved to exist. The
order variation is not the coefficientwise envelope
$\sum_\gamma\abs{\sigma_\gamma}t^\gamma$: for $\sigma=(1-t)\lambda$ the former is
$(1-t)\lambda$ and the latter $(1+t)\lambda$.

\begin{corollary}[Lattice structure; source 16]\label{meas:cw:cor:lattice}
The countable-scale measures on $(X,\Sigma)$ form a vector lattice over the
ordered field $\Kc$, with
$\sigma\vee\tau=\frac12(\sigma+\tau+\abs{\sigma-\tau}_{\mathrm{ord}})$ and
$\sigma\wedge\tau=\frac12(\sigma+\tau-\abs{\sigma-\tau}_{\mathrm{ord}})$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
The union of the two countable coefficient families has a common control.
Relative to it, the order of measures is the almost-everywhere pointwise order
of densities (Lemma~\ref{meas:cw:lem:sign}); take pointwise maxima and minima of
the densities.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Constructing positive measures}

Start with a finite positive measure $\lambda$ and a countable well-ordered
family $(h_\gamma)$ in $L^1(\lambda)$. If $h=\sum h_\gamma t^\gamma\ge0$ almost
everywhere, then $A\mapsto\intc_Ah\,d\lambda$ is a positive countable-scale
measure; the density is a positivity certificate. A robust choice is
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:unit-density}
 h=1+\sum_{n\ge1}a_nt^n,\qquad a_n\in L^1(\lambda),
\end{equation}
whose value at every point has leading coefficient $1$, whatever the signs and
sizes of the later coefficients; dividing by the total mass gives a
probability. Bounds on the $a_n$ are needed later for conditioning
(Theorem~\ref{meas:cw:thm:unit-conditional}), not for positivity. Another
construction is a \emph{positive hierarchy} $\mu=\sum_\gamma t^\gamma\rho_\gamma$ with
finite positive real measures $\rho_\gamma$ on a countable well-ordered index set,
mutually singular or overlapping: disjoint strata, bulk, surface and point
measures, mixtures of ordinary laws. Normalizing a hierarchy can create signed
coefficients although the hierarchy itself had positive ones.

\begin{proposition}[Extension from a generating algebra; source 16]
\label{meas:cw:prop:extension}
Let an algebra $\mathcal A_0$ generate $\Sigma$, let $W$ be countable and well
ordered, and suppose that for each $\gamma\in W$ a real signed premeasure
$p_\gamma$ on $\mathcal A_0$ has a unique finite signed extension $\bar p_\gamma$ to
$\Sigma$. Then $\sum_\gamma\bar p_\gamma t^\gamma$ is the unique coefficientwise extension
of the Hahn-valued data $\sum_\gamma p_\gamma t^\gamma$. It is positive exactly when its
Hahn density relative to a control is nonnegative almost everywhere.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Existence is the definition, since every value has support in $W$ and every
coefficient is countably additive. A competing coefficientwise extension agrees
coefficient by coefficient on $\mathcal A_0$, including at exponents where the
data vanish, and the assumed uniqueness of finite signed extensions gives
equality. Positivity is Theorem~\ref{meas:cw:thm:representation}.
\end{proof}

The hypothesis holds, for instance, when each premeasure is a difference of
two finite positive premeasures, to which Carath\'eodory's theorem applies; the
proposition deliberately does not replace it by bare finite additivity. On
$E^{\N}$ with its cylinder algebra every finitely additive set function is a
premeasure (a clopen set is not a countably infinite disjoint union of nonempty
clopen sets, by compactness), and the per-exponent hypothesis is the bounded
variation condition \eqref{meas:eq:var-test}. So for countable $W$ the
proposition, given that condition, recovers the existence and uniqueness in
Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:extension}; the theorem itself proves that the condition is
exact and computes the norm. Positive data on the algebra do not give a positive extension.
Source~16 shows this on Cantor space with $p=\rho+t(\rho-\delta_{x_*})$, for the
fair Bernoulli probability $\rho$: positive on every nonempty cylinder event,
total mass $1$, extension mass $-t$ at $\{x_*\}$. It notes that this two-scale
obstruction is already this report's; it is the mechanism of
Example~\ref{meas:ex:negative}, printed there once. Positivity must be checked on
the generated $\sigma$-algebra, or secured by a density certificate; finite
tests cannot replace that obligation.

\section{Integration of Hahn-valued functions}\label{meas:cw:sec:integral}

The integrals of Sections~\ref{meas:sec:integration}
and~\ref{meas:sec:coef-complex} integrate functions with values in the
coefficient field. Source~16 integrates Hahn-valued functions against
countable-scale measures.

\subsection{Common-support functions and a first integral}

A \emph{common-support Hahn function} is a pointwise series
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:common-support}
 f(x)=\sum_{\alpha\in S}f_\alpha(x)\,t^\alpha,
\end{equation}
with one countable well-ordered $S$ and measurable real coefficients $f_\alpha$.
Countable unions and intersections make its sign sets measurable. Its pointwise
absolute value is again a common-support function with support $S$ and
coefficients $\operatorname{sgn}(f(x))f_\alpha(x)$; the actual support at a point may
be smaller than $S$. For a countable-scale $\mu=\sum_{\gamma\in W}\mu_\gamma t^\gamma$, a
convenient sufficient integrability condition is
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:rectangular}
 \int_X\abs{f_\alpha}\,d\abs{\mu_\gamma}<\infty\qquad(\alpha\in S,\ \gamma\in W),
\end{equation}
and under it
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:first-integral}
 \int_Xf\,d\mu=\sum_{\delta\in S+W}\Bigl(\sum_{\alpha+\gamma=\delta}
 \int_Xf_\alpha\,d\mu_\gamma\Bigr)t^\delta .
\end{equation}
Each inner sum is finite and $S+W$ is well ordered and countable
(Lemma~\ref{meas:lem:sumalgebra}); no uncontrolled interchange of infinite sums
occurs. Every common-support function with bounded real coefficients satisfies
\eqref{meas:cw:eq:rectangular}, with no bound uniform in $\alpha$. For
$S=\{0\}$ and bounded $f$, \eqref{meas:cw:eq:first-integral} is the integral
\eqref{meas:eq:coef-integral} of bounded ordinary observables.

\begin{proposition}[Consistency and positivity; source 16]
\label{meas:cw:prop:first-integral}
The integral \eqref{meas:cw:eq:first-integral} is $\Kc$-linear on its domain,
agrees with finite simple integration, and agrees with ordinary integration for
a real measure and a real integrable function. If $\mu$ is positive and
$f\ge0$ almost everywhere, then $\int f\,d\mu\ge0$; moreover
$\abs{\int f\,d\mu}\le\int\abs f\,d\mu$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
With $d\mu_\gamma=\theta_\gamma\,d\lambda$, \eqref{meas:cw:eq:first-integral} becomes
$\intc f\theta\,d\lambda$, and \eqref{meas:cw:eq:rectangular} says that every
product coefficient $f_\alpha\theta_\gamma$ is $\lambda$-integrable. Linearity
involves finitely many terms at each exponent. If $f\ge0$, then $f\theta\ge0$
almost everywhere and Lemma~\ref{meas:cw:lem:sign} applies. Apply positivity to
$\abs f\pm f$ for the last inequality.
\end{proof}

\begin{example}[A derivative with no common well-ordered support; source 16]
\label{meas:cw:ex:rn-support}
On $X=\N$ with all subsets measurable put $\mu(A)=\sum_{n\in A}t^n$ and
$\nu(A)=\sum_{n\in A}2^{-n}$, both positive countable-scale measures with trivial
null ideals, so $\nu\ll\mu$. A density satisfying the integral identity on
singletons must be $f(n)=2^{-n}t^{-n}$. The union of these supports is
$\{-1,-2,\ldots\}$, not well ordered, so there is no common-support density. The
weighted values $f(n)\mu(\{n\})=2^{-n}$ are nevertheless summable
coefficientwise; the obstruction lies in the unweighted support of the
integrand, not in the desired measure.
\end{example}

Example~\ref{meas:ex:hahn-integrand} (source~10) uses the same unweighted
supports $t^{-n}$ against the strong probability with weights $t^n$, but there
the weighted values $g(n)w_n=1$ do not decay, so that integrand fails every
integrability condition of this section, not only the support condition. (This
comparison is added in the merge.) A theory insisting that every integrand have
a common support would have to state a restricted Radon--Nikodym theorem;
source~16 instead enlarges the domain in a way determined by the measure.

\subsection{The density-adapted integral}

Fix a nonzero positive countable-scale $\mu$ and a control $\lambda$ with the same
null sets (for instance \eqref{meas:cw:eq:control}); write $d\mu=\theta\,d\lambda$,
so $\theta>0$ almost everywhere. Let
\[
 \Hone{\lambda}=\Bigl\{h=\sum_{\delta\in T}h_\delta t^\delta:
 T\text{ countable well ordered},\ h_\delta\in L^1(\lambda)\Bigr\},
\]
regarded modulo equality outside one null set; countability again permits
simultaneous representatives.

\begin{definition}[Density-adapted integrability; source 16]
\label{meas:cw:def:adapted}
A function $f:X\to\Kc$ (modulo null sets) is \emph{$\mu$-integrable in the adapted
sense}, $f\in\Lad(\mu)$, if
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:adapted-domain}
 f=h/\theta\quad\lambda\text{-almost everywhere, for some }h\in\Hone{\lambda},
\end{equation}
and then
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:adapted-integral}
 \int_Af\,d\mu=\intc_Ah\,d\lambda=\sum_{\delta\in T}\Bigl(\int_Ah_\delta\,d\lambda\Bigr)t^\delta .
\end{equation}
The quotient is formed pointwise in $\Kc$ and is arbitrary on the null set
$\{\theta=0\}$. No common support is imposed on $f$ itself.
\end{definition}

This definition also fixes the class of measurable Hahn-valued functions in
use: an admissible integrand is presented by an integrable common-support
numerator and the positive common-support density. An arbitrary map into $\No$,
measurable for some order-Borel structure, is not automatically admissible.
Each pointwise quotient has countable support because $\Kc$ is a field; that
support may vary from point to point without a well-ordered union.

\begin{theorem}[Independence of the control; source 16]
\label{meas:cw:thm:control-invariant}
The class $\Lad(\mu)$, its almost-everywhere equivalence and the integrals
\eqref{meas:cw:eq:adapted-integral} do not depend on the choice of a finite
control with the same null sets as $\mu$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Let $\lambda'$ be another and $r=d\lambda/d\lambda'$, so $0<r<\infty$ almost
everywhere, and $\theta'=r\theta$. Then $f\theta'=r(f\theta)$ has the same pointwise
support, a real factor introducing no exponent, and for every coefficient
$\int\abs{rh_\delta}\,d\lambda'=\int\abs{h_\delta}\,d\lambda$ and
$\int_Arh_\delta\,d\lambda'=\int_Ah_\delta\,d\lambda$. Countably many coefficient
versions can be fixed on one common full-measure set.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Algebra, order and definiteness; source 16]
\label{meas:cw:thm:integral-properties}
$\Lad(\mu)$ is a $\Kc$-vector space closed under pointwise absolute value,
maximum and minimum. The integral is positive and $\Kc$-linear, and for $f\ge0$,
$\int f\,d\mu=0$ exactly when $f=0$ almost everywhere. The map
$f\mapsto\norm f_{1,\mu}=\int\abs f\,d\mu\in(\Kc)_{\ge0}$ is a definite
$\Kc$-valued norm: homogeneous, with the triangle inequality. Every function
satisfying \eqref{meas:cw:eq:rectangular} lies in $\Lad(\mu)$, with the same
integral.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Transport the operations through $f\mapsto f\theta$. A fixed scalar times an
$L^1$-coefficient numerator has integrable coefficients, by finite
convolution. Since $\theta>0$, $\abs f\theta=\abs{f\theta}$, which has the same
common support and coefficient magnitudes $\abs{h_\delta}$; maxima and minima
follow from absolute values. Lemma~\ref{meas:cw:lem:sign} gives positivity and
definiteness, and $\abs{f+g}\le\abs f+\abs g$ gives the triangle inequality. The
last assertion is the product-density computation in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{meas:cw:prop:first-integral}.
\end{proof}

``Norm'' means a norm with values in an ordered field. It is not asserted that
$\Lad(\mu)$ is a real Banach space, nor that order-bounded functions form an
integrability ideal; the latter is false (Example~\ref{meas:cw:ex:bounded-fail}).

For $f\in\Lad(\mu)$ the set function
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:nu-f}
 \nu_f(A)=\int_Af\,d\mu
\end{equation}
is a countable-scale measure with coefficient measures $h_\delta\lambda$, absolutely
continuous with respect to $\mu$. It is positive exactly when $f\ge0$ almost
everywhere, and by Theorem~\ref{meas:cw:thm:jordan}, applied with the sign sets of
$h$,
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:weighted-variation}
 \abs{\nu_f}_{\mathrm{ord}}(A)=\int_A\abs f\,d\mu .
\end{equation}

\section{Radon--Nikodym, Lebesgue decomposition, and convergence}
\label{meas:cw:sec:rn}

For a positive $\mu$ and a countable-scale $\nu$, $\nu\ll\mu$ means that
$\mu(A)=0$ implies $\nu(A)=0$. Since the subsets of a $\mu$-null set are
$\mu$-null, every coefficient measure of $\nu$ then vanishes on the whole null
ideal of $\mu$.

\begin{theorem}[Adapted Radon--Nikodym theorem; source 16]\label{meas:cw:thm:rn}
Let $\mu$ be a nonzero positive countable-scale measure and $\nu$ a
countable-scale measure on the same $\sigma$-algebra. Then $\nu\ll\mu$ if and only
if $\nu(A)=\int_Af\,d\mu$ for every $A$, for some $f\in\Lad(\mu)$. The density is
unique almost everywhere, and it is nonnegative exactly when $\nu$ is positive.
Thus $f\mapsto\nu_f$ is an order-preserving $\Kc$-linear bijection from $\Lad(\mu)$
onto the countable-scale measures absolutely continuous with respect to $\mu$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Take a control $\lambda$ with the null sets of $\mu$ and $d\mu=\theta\,d\lambda$. If
$\nu\ll\mu$, each coefficient $\nu_\delta$ is absolutely continuous with respect to
$\lambda$, so $\nu_\delta=n_\delta\lambda$ with $n_\delta\in L^1(\lambda)$; with the common
support of $\nu$, $n=\sum n_\delta t^\delta\in\Hone{\lambda}$, and $f=n/\theta$ has the
required integrals. Positivity follows from the sign lemma and $\theta>0$.
Conversely \eqref{meas:cw:eq:nu-f} has $\lambda$-integrable coefficient densities,
so it vanishes on $\mu$-null sets. If $f,g$ give the same measure, every
coefficient of $(f-g)\theta$ has zero integral on every set and so vanishes almost
everywhere; there are countably many, so $(f-g)\theta=0$ almost everywhere, and
$\theta>0$ gives $f=g$.
\end{proof}

In Example~\ref{meas:cw:ex:rn-support} take $\lambda(\{n\})=2^{-n}$. Then
$\theta(n)=2^nt^n$, the density of $\nu$ relative to $\lambda$ is $1$, and the adapted
derivative is $1/\theta(n)=2^{-n}t^{-n}$ with weighted numerator exactly $1$. The
theorem repairs the support obstruction rather than overlooking it.

\begin{theorem}[Lebesgue decomposition; source 16]\label{meas:cw:thm:lebesgue}
For positive countable-scale measures $\mu,\nu$ there are unique positive
countable-scale measures $\nu_{\mathrm{ac}},\nu_{\mathrm s}$ with
$\nu=\nu_{\mathrm{ac}}+\nu_{\mathrm s}$, $\nu_{\mathrm{ac}}\ll\mu$, and
$\nu_{\mathrm s}$ concentrated on a measurable $\mu$-null set. When $\mu\ne0$, the
absolutely continuous part has a unique density in $\Lad(\mu)$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Let $\lambda$ be the sum of controls of $\mu$ and $\nu$ and $\theta,n\ge0$ their
densities. Put $E=\{\theta>0\}$; by the sign lemma $\mu(A)=0$ exactly when $\theta=0$
almost everywhere on $A$. Define $\nu_{\mathrm{ac}}$ and $\nu_{\mathrm s}$ by the
densities $n\ind_E$ and $n\ind_{E^c}$; positivity, additivity and support are
immediate, $E^c$ is $\mu$-null, and $\mu(A)=0$ makes $A\cap E$ $\lambda$-null, so
$\nu_{\mathrm{ac}}(A)=0$. For uniqueness, the absolutely continuous parts of two
decompositions vanish on the union of their singular carriers, which is
$\mu$-null, and on its complement both singular parts vanish; restrict to the two
pieces. Theorem~\ref{meas:cw:thm:rn} gives the density. If $\mu=0$ the
decomposition is $0+\nu$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{A bounded unit-leading special case.} Let $\Gamma=\Z$, $\lambda$ finite, and
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:bounded-unit}
 d\mu=w\,d\lambda,\qquad w=1+\sum_{n\ge1}a_nt^n,\qquad a_n\in L^\infty(\lambda).
\end{equation}
Every coefficient of $w^{-1}$ is a polynomial in finitely many $a_j$, hence
bounded. Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:domain-unit}
 \Lad(\mu)=L^1(\lambda)((t))
\end{equation}
as sets of function classes: series with one integer lower bound on their
exponents and integrable real coefficients, a class preserved by multiplication
by $w$ and $w^{-1}$. So every countable-scale measure absolutely continuous with
respect to $\lambda$ has a common-support density relative to $\mu$. The
boundedness matters: an $L^1$ coefficient need not have an integrable square.
In particular the interior extensions of Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:interior}, whose
density coefficients $H_\rho$ are in $L^2(P_0)$ and in general not bounded, are not
automatically in this special case.

\subsection{Convergence: valid theorems and sharp warnings}

\begin{theorem}[Coefficientwise dominated convergence; source 16]\label{meas:cw:thm:dct}
Let $f_n\in\Lad(\mu)$ and $h_n=f_n\theta$, all with support in one countable
well-ordered $T$. Suppose that for every $\delta\in T$, $h_{n,\delta}\to h_\delta$
almost everywhere and $\abs{h_{n,\delta}}\le H_\delta$ with $H_\delta\in L^1(\lambda)$. Then
$h=\sum h_\delta t^\delta\in\Hone{\lambda}$, $f=h/\theta\in\Lad(\mu)$,
$\int f_n\,d\mu\tocoef\int f\,d\mu$, and $\norm{f_n-f}_{1,\mu}\tocoef0$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Scalar dominated convergence gives $L^1$ convergence at every exponent, and the
common support stays $T$. The $\delta$-coefficient of $\abs{h_n-h}$ is
$\operatorname{sgn}(h_n-h)(h_{n,\delta}-h_\delta)$, whose integral is bounded in absolute
value by $\int\abs{h_{n,\delta}-h_\delta}\,d\lambda\to0$.
\end{proof}

For common-support $f_n$ with one support, a stronger but easily checked
sufficient hypothesis is coefficientwise almost-everywhere convergence and a
separate integrable majorant for each pair of function and measure coefficients
in \eqref{meas:cw:eq:rectangular}. Neither statement concerns limits in the
surreal order. For real functions $0\le f_n\uparrow f$ with $\int f\,d\abs{\mu_\gamma}<\infty$
for every $\gamma$, ordinary monotone convergence followed by signed dominated
convergence gives the expected coefficientwise limit; a weighted numerator whose
every coefficient increases nonnegatively has a monotone-convergence principle
