\emph{provided every limiting coefficient integral is finite}. This does not
include arbitrary order-monotone Hahn functions.

\begin{example}[A bounded function outside the domain; source 16]
\label{meas:cw:ex:bounded-fail}
Let $X=(0,1]$ with Lebesgue measure $\lambda$, a constant Hahn measure. At every
$x$, $0<f(x)=t/x<1$; yet $f\notin\Lad(\lambda)$, since its $t$-coefficient $1/x$ is
not integrable. An integrable constant dominates, in the field order, a
nonintegrable Hahn-valued function.
\end{example}

\begin{example}[Bounded convergence fails with eventual pointwise equality;
source 16]\label{meas:cw:ex:bct-fail}
On the same space let $f_n(x)=t\min\{n,1/x\}$. Every $f_n$ has bounded
coefficients and lies in the domain; $0\le f_n\le1$, the sequence increases, and
at each fixed $x$ it is eventually equal to $t/x$, so it converges even in the
order topology of $\No$. But $\int_0^1f_n\,dx=t(1+\log n)$, splitting the real
integral at $1/n$, and the $t$-coefficient diverges: no coefficientwise limit
exists. The hypotheses of bounded and dominated convergence, read in the surreal
order alone, are insufficient. Replacing the divergent coefficient by a chosen
infinite surreal would be an additional regularization rule, not a consequence
of this theory.
\end{example}

\begin{example}[The positive cone is not coefficientwise closed; source 16]
\label{meas:cw:ex:positive-not-closed}
Each $a_n=1/n-t$ is positive, but $a_n\tocoef-t<0$; so positive functions, and
positive measures such as $(1/n-t)\lambda$, can have a negative coefficientwise
limit. Normalization does not help: on a two-point space the weights $1/n-t$ and
$1-1/n+t$ form a probability for every $n\ge2$, with negative limiting first
weight. This does not contradict the sign lemma, which concerns one function or
measure; positivity of a limit must be verified separately. It is the
measure-level counterpart of the quantifier distinction in
Section~\ref{meas:sec:coef-examples} (``No ordinary continuity in the field
order'').
\end{example}

\paragraph{Support-controlled completeness.} For a fixed countable well-ordered
$T=\{\delta_1,\delta_2,\ldots\}$ the numerators with support in $T$ form the product
$\prod_{\delta\in T}L^1(\lambda)$, complete for its product locally convex topology,
with compatible metric
$d(h,g)=\sum_k2^{-k}\min\{1,\norm{h_{\delta_k}-g_{\delta_k}}_{L^1(\lambda)}\}$. Through
$f\mapsto f\theta$ this gives a complete support-controlled space of adapted
functions, whose coefficient seminorms do not depend on the control (as in
Theorem~\ref{meas:cw:thm:control-invariant}). No global completeness is claimed
once the support may vary.

\section{Products, Fubini, and quadratic inequalities}
\label{meas:cw:sec:products}

\begin{theorem}[Product measure; source 16]\label{meas:cw:thm:product}
Let $\mu=\sum_{\alpha\in S}\mu_\alpha t^\alpha$ and $\nu=\sum_{\beta\in T}\nu_\beta t^\beta$ be positive
countable-scale measures on $(X,\Sigma)$ and $(Y,\mathcal T)$. There is a unique
coefficientwise measure on $\Sigma\otimes\mathcal T$ with
$(\mu\otimes\nu)(A\times B)=\mu(A)\nu(B)$, namely
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:product}
 \mu\otimes\nu=\sum_{\delta\in S+T}\Bigl(\sum_{\alpha+\beta=\delta}\mu_\alpha\otimes\nu_\beta\Bigr)t^\delta ,
\end{equation}
and it is positive on every product-measurable set.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Each coefficient in \eqref{meas:cw:eq:product} is a finite sum of ordinary finite
signed product measures, with support in the countable well-ordered $S+T$. With
controls $\lambda,\rho$ and densities $\theta,\zeta\ge0$, the product has density
$\theta(x)\zeta(y)$ relative to $\lambda\otimes\rho$, every coefficient being integrable
since $\iint\abs{\theta_\alpha(x)\zeta_\beta(y)}\,d\lambda\,d\rho=
\norm{\theta_\alpha}_{L^1(\lambda)}\norm{\zeta_\beta}_{L^1(\rho)}$. The density is nonnegative
almost everywhere, so the sign lemma gives positivity on all product-measurable
sets, not merely on rectangles. Uniqueness holds exponent by exponent, by
uniqueness of finite signed measures agreeing on rectangles.
\end{proof}

Iteration gives associative finite products and finite-dimensional
independence. No countably infinite product theorem is inferred: infinite
products need separate uniform coefficient control, which is the subject of
Theorems~\ref{meas:thm:interior}--\ref{meas:thm:mixed}. When $S$ is countable, the
two factors in the proof of Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:mixed} are countable-scale
measures (their exponents lie in the countable monoid $S^*$), and
Theorem~\ref{meas:cw:thm:product} gives a second proof of the positivity of
\eqref{meas:eq:mixed-tensor} on all Borel sets, without the section formula
\eqref{meas:eq:mixed-sections}. That proof via sections needs no countability.
(This observation is added in the merge.)

\begin{theorem}[Adapted Fubini theorem; source 16]\label{meas:cw:thm:fubini}
Let $\mu,\nu$ be nonzero positive countable-scale measures and
$f\in\Lad(\mu\otimes\nu)$. For almost every $x$ the section $f(x,\cdot)$ lies in
$\Lad(\nu)$; its integral, as a function of $x$, lies in $\Lad(\mu)$; and
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:fubini}
 \int f\,d(\mu\otimes\nu)=\int_X\Bigl(\int_Yf(x,y)\,d\nu(y)\Bigr)d\mu(x).
\end{equation}
The same holds with $X$ and $Y$ interchanged.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
With the controls and densities above write
$h(x,y)=f(x,y)\theta(x)\zeta(y)=\sum_{\delta\in U}h_\delta(x,y)t^\delta$, with $U$ countable
well ordered and every $h_\delta\in L^1(\lambda\otimes\rho)$. Scalar Fubini gives,
outside one $\lambda$-null set, integrable sections of every coefficient and
$G_\delta(x)=\int h_\delta(x,y)\,d\rho(y)\in L^1(\lambda)$. Fix such an $x$ with
$\theta(x)>0$. The weighted section $f(x,y)\zeta(y)=h(x,y)/\theta(x)$ is a Hahn function
of $y$ with integrable coefficients times the fixed scalar $\theta(x)^{-1}$; its
support lies in $U+\supp(\theta(x)^{-1})$ and each coefficient is a finite linear
combination of integrable sections, so it is admissible for $\nu$. Its integral is
$G(x)/\theta(x)$ with $G=\sum G_\delta t^\delta\in\Hone{\lambda}$, admissible for $\mu$, with
outer integral $\intc G\,d\lambda$. Scalar Fubini at each exponent gives
\eqref{meas:cw:eq:fubini}.
\end{proof}

The theorem assumes coefficientwise absolute integrability of the \emph{weighted
joint} function, through membership in the adapted domain. A nonnegative Hahn
function does not satisfy a universal Tonelli theorem with values in $\Kc$:
Example~\ref{meas:cw:ex:bounded-fail} already has a divergent coefficient. If all
weighted coefficients are nonnegative, ordinary Tonelli applies coefficient by
coefficient, and a Hahn-valued answer exists when all resulting coefficient
integrals are finite.

\paragraph{Quadratic inequalities without a Hilbert space.} Let $\Hinf{\lambda}$ be
the common-support Hahn functions with individually bounded real coefficients,
modulo $\lambda$-null equality; it is a $\Kc$-algebra. For a positive $\mu$ with
control $\lambda$ put $\inner fg_\mu=\int fg\,d\mu$ for $f,g\in\Hinf{\lambda}$. All
coefficient products are integrable, the form is positive definite, and
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:cs}
 \abs{\inner fg_\mu}^2\le\inner ff_\mu\,\inner gg_\mu :
\end{equation}
if $\inner gg_\mu>0$, apply positivity to $\inner{f-cg}{f-cg}_\mu$ with
$c=\inner fg_\mu/\inner gg_\mu\in\Kc$; if it is $0$, then $g=0$ almost everywhere. No
real closure or square root is used. For complex coefficients put
$\inner fg=\int f\overline g\,d\mu$, integrating real and imaginary parts; the same
argument gives the Hermitian inequality, with values in $\Kc(i)$ and, through
Convention~\ref{meas:conv:surreal}, in the surcomplex numbers. There is no order
on the surcomplex field; positivity refers to the squared modulus and the
underlying positive measure. This gives finite Gram-matrix algebra; it is not a
complete Hilbert space, and no spectral theorem for arbitrary operators is
claimed. The Hermitian moment forms of Section~\ref{meas:sec:hermitian} are
different objects: forms of a functional on polynomials, which there need not
come from any positive strong measure.

\section{Probability: standard part, conditioning, conditional expectation}
\label{meas:cw:sec:probability}

\subsection{Normalization, the real shadow, and conditioning on events}

Every nonzero positive countable-scale measure normalizes to a probability
$P=\mu/\mu(X)$, even when $\mu(X)$ is infinite or infinitesimal; scalar
multiplication preserves the class, positivity and the null ideal. That a
coefficientwise probability has no negative-exponent coefficient, that $P_0$ is an
ordinary probability, and that $\st P(A)=P_0(A)$ is
Remark~\ref{meas:rem:probability-exponents}; source~16 re-derived it as its
``classical shadow'' theorem, printed here once. The shadow need not detect
all null sets: a point of positive infinitesimal mass has shadow zero without
being $P$-null. Standard-part reduction forgets exactly the information the
further scales were meant to keep, and it differs from taking standard parts of
an arbitrary finitely additive non-Archimedean probability, which need not be
countably additive (Section~\ref{meas:cw:sub:numerosity}). In the strong class the
shadow is finitely supported (Trap~2 of Section~\ref{meas:sub:traps}).

For an event $B$ with $P(B)>0$, $P(\,\cdot\mid B)=P(\,\cdot\cap B)/P(B)$ is again a
coefficientwise probability: this is Proposition~\ref{meas:prop:conditioning}
(source~15), which source~16 states for countable scales, with values in $[0,1]$
and finite Bayes identities and the multiplication rule following from field
algebra. There is no need for $P_0(B)>0$. Source~16 adds the leading-scale
description.

\begin{proposition}[Conditioning activates the first layer that sees the event;
source 16]\label{meas:cw:prop:leading}
Let $P(B)>0$ and $\beta=v(P(B))$. If $P(A\cap B)\ne0$ then $v(P(A\cap B))\ge\beta$ and
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:leading}
 \st P(A\mid B)=\begin{cases}
 \lc P(A\cap B)/\lc P(B),& v(P(A\cap B))=\beta,\\
 0,& v(P(A\cap B))>\beta .\end{cases}
\end{equation}
For a positive hierarchy $\mu=\sum_\gamma t^\gamma\rho_\gamma$ and $P=\mu/\mu(X)$, let $\beta$ be
the first index with $\rho_\beta(B)>0$. Then
$\st P(A\mid B)=\rho_\beta(A\cap B)/\rho_\beta(B)$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Monotonicity gives $0\le P(A\cap B)\le P(B)$, hence the valuation inequality, and
\eqref{meas:cw:eq:leading} is the leading-term quotient. For a hierarchy every
coefficient $\rho_\gamma(B)$ is nonnegative, so $v(\mu(B))=\beta$ with leading coefficient
$\rho_\beta(B)$, while $\rho_\gamma(A\cap B)\le\rho_\gamma(B)=0$ for $\gamma<\beta$.
\end{proof}

Example~\ref{meas:ex:conditioning} (source~15) is an instance: conditioning on at
least one rare success activates the $t^\alpha$ layer $\sum_jc_j\delta_{e_j}$. The
formula resembles lexicographic probability; exact coefficientwise
$\sigma$-additivity and the integration domain are additional structure, and the
relations among lexicographic, Popper and non-Archimedean probability are
Brickhill and Horsten's~\cite{BH}.

\begin{example}[A diffuse law with two infinitesimal atoms; source 16]
\label{meas:cw:ex:worked}
On $[0,1]$ with Lebesgue probability $\lambda$ put
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:worked}
 \mu=\lambda+t\delta_0+t^2\delta_1,\qquad P=\frac{\mu}{1+t+t^2}.
\end{equation}
For real bounded measurable $f$,
$\E_Pf=\bigl(\int_0^1f(x)\,dx+tf(0)+t^2f(1)\bigr)/(1+t+t^2)$. The shadow is
Lebesgue measure. For $B=\{0,1\}$, $P(\{1\}\mid B)=t/(1+t)$ and
$P(\{0\}\mid B)=1/(1+t)$, defined although $P_0(B)=0$. For the identity function,
\[
 \E_PX=\frac{1/2+t^2}{1+t+t^2}=\frac12-\frac12t+t^2-\frac12t^3-\frac12t^4+\cdots ;
\]
the exact rational expression, not a truncation, is the expectation. A control
is $\lambda+\delta_0+\delta_1$, relative to which the density of $\mu$ is $1$ on the
diffuse part, $t$ at $0$ and $t^2$ at $1$: the strata are explicit.
\end{example}

\begin{proposition}[A first Borel--Cantelli lemma; source 16]
\label{meas:cw:prop:borel-cantelli}
If events $A_n$ satisfy $\sum_n\abs{P_\gamma}(A_n)<\infty$ for every $\gamma$, then
$P(\limsup_nA_n)=0$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The scalar lemma for each finite positive measure $\abs{P_\gamma}$ makes the limsup
null for every coefficient, hence for the control, hence for $P$.
\end{proof}
The hypothesis is coefficient-variation summability, not an unqualified
summability of event probabilities.

\subsection{Conditional expectation}

Let $\mathcal G\subseteq\Sigma$ be a sub-$\sigma$-algebra and $f\in\Lad(\mu)$. The
measure $\nu_f$ of \eqref{meas:cw:eq:nu-f} restricts to $\mathcal G$ and stays
absolutely continuous with respect to $\mu|_{\mathcal G}$, so
Theorem~\ref{meas:cw:thm:rn} gives a unique adapted derivative \emph{on the
restricted measurable space}.

\begin{theorem}[Conditional expectation on the restricted space; source 16]
\label{meas:cw:thm:conditional}
There is a unique $\E_\mu[f\mid\mathcal G]\in\Lad(\mu|_{\mathcal G})$ with
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:conditional}
 \int_A\E_\mu[f\mid\mathcal G]\,d(\mu|_{\mathcal G})=\int_Af\,d\mu\qquad(A\in\mathcal G).
\end{equation}
With a control $\lambda$ with the null sets of $\mu$, $\theta=d\mu/d\lambda$ and
$h=f\theta$,
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:conditional-ratio}
 \E_\mu[f\mid\mathcal G]=\frac{\E_\lambda[h\mid\mathcal G]}{\E_\lambda[\theta\mid\mathcal G]},
\end{equation}
numerator and denominator being ordinary conditional expectations taken
coefficient by coefficient; the denominator is positive almost everywhere. The
operator is $\Kc$-linear and positive, and for $\mathcal G'\subseteq\mathcal G$,
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:tower}
 \E_{\mu|_{\mathcal G}}\bigl[\E_\mu[f\mid\mathcal G]\mid\mathcal G'\bigr]=\E_\mu[f\mid\mathcal G'] .
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Coefficientwise scalar conditional expectations of $h$ and $\theta$ keep their
countable common supports and integrable coefficients; they are the densities of
$\nu_f|_{\mathcal G}$ and $\mu|_{\mathcal G}$ relative to $\lambda|_{\mathcal G}$. For
$A\in\mathcal G$, $\mu(A)=0$ exactly when $\lambda(A)=0$, so the density of
$\mu|_{\mathcal G}$ is positive almost everywhere by the sign lemma, and the ratio is
the adapted derivative. Linearity, positivity and uniqueness come from
Theorem~\ref{meas:cw:thm:rn}. Both sides of \eqref{meas:cw:eq:tower} represent
$\nu_f|_{\mathcal G'}$.
\end{proof}

Quotients of two common-support functions have measurable coefficient
functions: partition the space by the countably many possible leading exponents
of the denominator and use finite convolution and formal inversion on each
piece. The union of possible exponents is countable but need not be well
ordered. This is what measurability of the representatives in
\eqref{meas:cw:eq:conditional-ratio} means; there is no appeal to a Borel
structure on the proper class $\No$. The theorem does \emph{not} assert
$\E_\mu[f\mid\mathcal G]\in\Lad(\mu)$ on the original $\sigma$-algebra: integrating
on the coarser space can allow cancellation within a fibre before absolute
integrability is tested.

\begin{example}[Conditioning leaves the original domain; source 16]
\label{meas:cw:ex:conditional-fail}
Let $X=\N\times\{0,1\}$ with all subsets measurable,
$\lambda(\{(n,b)\})=c/(2n^3)$ with $c=(\sum_nn^{-3})^{-1}$, $a(n,0)=0$, $a(n,1)=n$, and
$d\mu=(1+ta)\,d\lambda$. Since $\sum_nn^{-2}<\infty$, $a$ is integrable and $\mu$ is a
positive two-scale measure. Let $f=\ind_{\{b=0\}}$, bounded and in $\Lad(\mu)$, and
let $\mathcal G$ consist of the unions of fibres $\{n\}\times\{0,1\}$. On fibre $n$
the scalar conditional expectations are $1+\frac n2t$ and $\frac12$, so the only
possible fibre-constant derivative is
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:bad-conditional}
 g_n=\frac1{2+nt}=\frac12-\frac n4t+\frac{n^2}8t^2-\cdots ,
\end{equation}
which lies in $\Lad(\mu|_{\mathcal G})$ because its weighted numerator is
$g_n(1+\frac n2t)=\frac12$. On the full space, the $t^2$-coefficient of
$g(1+ta)$ is $n^2/8$ at $(n,0)$ and $-n^2/8$ at $(n,1)$, with absolute integral
$\sum_n\frac c{n^3}\frac{n^2}8=\frac c8\sum_n\frac1n=\infty$. So $g\notin\Lad(\mu)$,
and since every $\mathcal G$-measurable candidate equals
\eqref{meas:cw:eq:bad-conditional}, no version lies in the full domain. The
function $g$ has support in $\{0,1,2,\ldots\}$: the obstruction is not a lack of
common support but coefficientwise absolute integrability on the finer
$\sigma$-algebra.
\end{example}

A conditional expectation is therefore, in general, a restricted-space
Radon--Nikodym derivative, not an endomorphism of one global $L^1$ space.

\begin{theorem}[Bounded-coefficient perturbative conditioning; source 16]
\label{meas:cw:thm:unit-conditional}
Let $\lambda$ be a real probability, $\Gamma=\Z$, and $d\mu=w\,d\lambda$ with $w$ as in
\eqref{meas:cw:eq:bounded-unit}. On the algebra
$\mathscr A=L^\infty(\lambda)((t))$ the conditional expectation
\eqref{meas:cw:eq:conditional-ratio} takes values in
$L^\infty(\lambda|_{\mathcal G})((t))\subseteq\Lad(\mu)$. It preserves constants, is
positive and $\Kc$-linear, satisfies the tower and idempotence identities, and
satisfies $\E_\mu[uf\mid\mathcal G]=u\,\E_\mu[f\mid\mathcal G]$ for $u$ in the bounded
$\mathcal G$-measurable algebra $L^\infty(\lambda|_{\mathcal G})((t))$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The denominator is $1+\sum_n\E_\lambda[a_n\mid\mathcal G]t^n$, with bounded
coefficients and an inverse with bounded coefficients. For $f\in\mathscr A$ the
numerator $\E_\lambda[fw\mid\mathcal G]$ has bounded coefficients and an integer lower
bound on its support, and finite convolution gives the membership; by
\eqref{meas:cw:eq:domain-unit} the result may be integrated against $\mu$ on the full
$\sigma$-algebra, its coefficient products with those of $w$ being integrable. The
defining identity follows from the scalar conditional identity exponent by
exponent. Tower, idempotence and constants follow from uniqueness, all functions
now lying in the full domain. For pull-out, the coefficients of $u$ are bounded and
$\mathcal G$-measurable, so scalar pull-out and finite convolution give
$\E_\lambda[ufw\mid\mathcal G]=u\,\E_\lambda[fw\mid\mathcal G]$; divide by the same
denominator. Positivity is inherited from
Theorem~\ref{meas:cw:thm:conditional}.
\end{proof}

For real bounded $f$ and $w=1+ta+t^2b+\cdots$, with $F=\E[f\mid\mathcal G]$,
$A=\E[a\mid\mathcal G]$, $B=\E[b\mid\mathcal G]$ (all under $\lambda$),
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:covariance}
 \E_\mu[f\mid\mathcal G]=F+t\bigl(\E[fa\mid\mathcal G]-FA\bigr)
 +t^2\bigl(\E[fb\mid\mathcal G]-A\,\E[fa\mid\mathcal G]+F(A^2-B)\bigr)+\cdots,
\end{equation}
where the omitted terms are an exact formal remainder with exponents at least
$3$. The $t$-coefficient is a conditional covariance. These are exact
perturbative Bayesian updates at successive scales, not approximations requiring
a numerical value of $t$.

\paragraph{Two certificates.} In symbolic computation source~16 recommends
storing a common support descriptor with symbolic coefficient measures, and
attaching to every result two certificates: a support certificate and an
absolute-integrability certificate.
Example~\ref{meas:cw:ex:rn-support} fails the first for an unweighted integrand;
Example~\ref{meas:cw:ex:conditional-fail} fails the second after conditioning. A
system reporting only a formal quotient would miss both.

\section{Surreal masses, charts, equal point masses, and the countability
boundary}\label{meas:cw:sec:boundary}

\subsection{Infinite and infinitesimal masses; charts; strata}

If $\lambda$ is a real probability, $t^{-1}\lambda$ is a countable-scale measure of
infinite total mass $t^{-1}$ and $t\lambda$ one of infinitesimal mass; both are
diffuse when $\lambda$ is. The class is not limited to finite surreal
probabilities. This does not assign the value $t^{-1}$ to an arbitrary divergent
real integral: the scale is part of the definition of the measure. Lebesgue
measure on all of $\R$ is not in the class with interval lengths unchanged, since
its exponent-zero coefficient would have infinite total mass; a local or
$\sigma$-finite extension needs further definitions, not the symbol $\omega$ in place
of $+\infty$.

Fix $a\in\Kc$ and $s\in(\Kc)_{>0}$. The map $\phi(u)=a+su$ sends $[0,1]\subseteq\R$
injectively into $\No$ (after Convention~\ref{meas:conv:surreal}). On
$X_{a,s}=\phi([0,1])$, with the transported Borel $\sigma$-algebra,
$\mu_{a,s}(\phi(A))=s\lambda(A)$ is a positive measure of total mass $s$, and for
admissible $f$, $\int_{X_{a,s}}f\,d\mu_{a,s}=s\int_0^1f(a+su)\,du$, by pushforward and
scalar multiplication. It is a measure on a set of surreal points. But $X_{a,s}$ is
\emph{not} the surreal interval $[a,a+s]$: it omits the parameters that are
nonreal surreals in $(0,1)$. Real parameter charts with surreal affine images
and chosen volume factors work in higher dimensions as well; consistency on
overlaps must be checked from the coordinate changes, and a canonical volume on
all surreal subsets does not follow.

On a compact region $D\subseteq\R^d$ with finite volume $\lambda_D$, a finite surface
measure $\sigma$ on a boundary or defect, and a finite atomic $\eta$,
$\mu=\lambda_D+t\sigma+t^2\eta$ keeps all three contributions: a surface invisible to
the standard part has positive conditional mass at the first infinitesimal scale
(Proposition~\ref{meas:cw:prop:leading}). More generally, for disjoint strata
$X_k$ with finite measures $\rho_k$ on them, $\sum_kt^{\gamma_k}\rho_k$ is a positive
hierarchy whenever the exponents form a countable well-ordered set, with no
decay rate imposed on the masses. A physical reading would need compatible units
and a model for the coefficients; no claim that surreal scalars regularize a
physical singularity follows from the algebra.

\subsection{Equal infinitesimal point masses need another axiom}
\label{meas:cw:sub:numerosity}

Coefficientwise measures keep classical countable additivity at each scale. A
different goal is to give \emph{the same} positive infinitesimal to every point
of an infinite sample space, with a probability defined on all subsets. The
following construction is of the standard type in the non-Archimedean
probability and numerosity tradition~\cite{BHW,BBDN}. Let $X$ be infinite and
$I$ the set of its nonempty finite subsets. The cones
$C_{F_0}=\{F\in I:F_0\subseteq F\}$, $F_0$ finite, have the finite intersection
property; choose an ultrafilter $\mathcal U$ on $I$ containing them all, and in the
ordered ultrapower field $\R^I/\mathcal U$ put
\begin{equation}\label{meas:cw:eq:numerosity}
 \mathfrak n(A)=\bigl[F\mapsto\abs{A\cap F}\bigr]_{\mathcal U},\qquad
 \mathfrak N=\mathfrak n(X),\qquad P(A)=\mathfrak n(A)/\mathfrak N .
\end{equation}

\begin{proposition}[Numerosity probability; source 16]
\label{meas:cw:prop:numerosity}
$\mathfrak n$ is finitely additive on disjoint sets, $\mathfrak n(\{x\})=1$, and
$\mathfrak N$ exceeds every positive integer. $P$ is a finitely additive probability
on all subsets of $X$, with $P(A)>0$ for every nonempty $A$ and
$P(\{x\})=\mathfrak N^{-1}$ for every $x$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Finite counting identities hold pointwise on $I$, hence in the ultrapower. The
cone requiring $x$ lies in $\mathcal U$, so $\mathfrak n(\{x\})=1$; a cone requiring $k+1$
distinct points shows $\mathfrak N>k$. If $x\in A$, then $\mathfrak n(A)\ge1$ on that
cone, so $P(A)\ge\mathfrak N^{-1}>0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Set-sized ordered fields embed in $\No$; classical]
\label{meas:cw:prop:realization}
Every set-sized ordered field extending $\R$ has an ordered-field embedding into
$\No$ fixing $\R$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}[Proof (source 16's sketch)]
Pass to the real closure and choose a well-ordered transcendence basis over $\R$.
Embed the real closures generated by initial segments. At a successor stage the
next basis element determines a cut in the preceding real closed field, a set;
the cut property of $\No$ supplies a surreal realizing the image cut, necessarily
outside the image and hence transcendental over it. Over a real closed field the
sign of a polynomial is determined by position relative to its roots, so the cut
determines an ordered embedding of the rational function field; extend to its
real closure inside $\No$, using real-closedness of $\No$~\cite{Conway,Gonshor}.
Take unions at limit stages.
\end{proof}

The foundations report records the caveats on universality claims
(\path{found:sub:universality}~\cite{repofound}). Applied to the ultrapower, the
proposition makes the probabilities of \eqref{meas:cw:eq:numerosity} genuine
surreal numbers; the infinitesimal $\mathfrak N^{-1}$ depends on the embedding and
is not automatically the $\omega^{-1}$ of earlier examples.

For countably infinite $X$ the construction satisfies none of the three
countable-sum axioms: at the common leading exponent of the masses
$\mathfrak N^{-1}$ the real coefficient series diverges (coefficientwise additivity
fails), infinitely many terms contribute there (strong summability fails; this
is the physics report's observation, Section~\ref{meas:sub:collection}), and
order-limit additivity fails by Corollary~\ref{meas:cw:cor:orderlimits}. Its finite
additivity is exact. Benci, Horsten and Wenmackers use a distinct
infinite-additivity axiom~\cite{BHW}, not to be conflated with any of these. On
$\N$ a free ultrafilter applied to the initial-segment frequencies,
$P(A)=[\abs{A\cap\{1,\ldots,n\}}/n]_{\mathcal U}$, gives $\st P(A)=d$ whenever $A$
has natural density $d$; but the standard-part set function on all subsets is not
countably additive, every singleton having standard part $0$. Loeb-type
procedures obtain countably additive real measures on suitably specified spaces
and $\sigma$-algebras; taking standard parts on an arbitrary fixed event algebra is
not such a procedure. Bottazzi develops a related real-valued measure theory on
non-Archimedean extensions~\cite{Bottazzi}. The collection's report
\path{surreal/finite-surreal-probability}~\cite{repofsp} treats finite probability
over surreal fields and keeps its infinite-space constructions (strong laws,
coefficientwise hierarchies, finitely additive extensions, internal and Loeb
models) apart.

\subsection{Why countably many scales are essential}

The coefficientwise definition makes sense for any well-ordered common support,
and Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:nullideal} is stated in that generality. The control
measure, and every proof above that takes a union of exceptional null sets, use
countability. This is a genuine boundary.

\begin{example}[Uncountably many positive atoms; source 16]\label{meas:cw:ex:omega1}
Let $X=\omega_1$ with all subsets measurable, let $\Gamma$ be the subgroup of $\No$
generated by the ordinals below $\omega_1$, and put $\mu(A)=\sum_{\alpha\in A}t^\alpha$ in
$\R((t^\Gamma))$. Every coefficient is a point mass $\delta_\alpha$, every disjoint
sequence is strongly summable (at each exponent at most one event contributes),
and every singleton has positive mass. No finite real measure has the same null
ideal, since it would have uncountably many atoms. The total mass is a valid
Hahn series outside $\Kc$.
\end{example}

This is a strong Hahn measure on a countably separated space (the points of
$\omega_1$ are separated by countably many sets, as $\omega_1$ injects into
$\{0,1\}^{\N}$), of the kind in Remark~\ref{meas:rem:many-atoms} (there
normalized by an anchor point), printed there once; its role here is to show that the control cannot be kept. Source~18's
\texttt{herg:ex:uncountable} (Remark~\ref{meas:rem:nullideal}) shows the same
boundary for the null-ideal criterion. The report
\path{surreal/finite-surreal-probability}~\cite{repofsp} gives, in its section on
arbitrary surreal-valued observables, a pointwise positive Hahn-valued function
with one uncountable well-ordered support whose coefficientwise integral against
an ordinary countably additive probability is negative, and notes that with
countable support positivity is preserved: that is the sharpness of the
implication (i)$\Rightarrow$(ii) of Lemma~\ref{meas:cw:lem:sign} in its countability
hypothesis. None of these refutes a theorem above. Uncountable support can be
studied with local controls, transfinite null ideals or another measurable
structure, but those are further theories.

\subsection{A comparison of frameworks}

The literature contains genuinely different non-Archimedean measure programs,
and source~16 compares its own with them. Benci, Horsten and Wenmackers replace
classical countable additivity by another infinite-additivity axiom~\cite{BHW};
elementary numerosities encode finite counting identities on infinite sets and
relate suitable standard parts to ordinary measures~\cite{BBDN}; Brickhill and
Horsten compare non-Archimedean, lexicographic and Popper probability~\cite{BH}.
Kaiser constructs measure and integration for semialgebraic sets and functions
over certain non-Archimedean real closed fields, with values in a controlled
extension, such as a polynomial ring, rather than the field itself~\cite{Kaiser}.
Work on the Levi-Civita field builds measure from interval covers and outer
measures adapted to that field, with a changed measurable-set
category~\cite{LeviCivita}. Bottazzi's measure is real-valued on a
non-Archimedean domain~\cite{Bottazzi}, whereas the measures here have a
non-Archimedean range on an ordinary measurable space.

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabularx}{\textwidth}{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.21\textwidth}
 >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}X>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}X
 >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}X@{}}
\toprule
Framework & Addition and limits & Diffuse real shadow & Equal infinitesimal
point masses\\
\midrule
Order limits in $\No$ & Set-indexed limits eventually constant
(Cor.~\ref{meas:cw:cor:orderlimits}) & No Lebesgue extension & No countable
lottery\\
Strong Hahn measures & Finitely many contributions per exponent & No: finitely
supported shadow on countably separated spaces (Cor.~\ref{meas:cor:shadow}) & No
countable lottery (Cor.~\ref{meas:cor:transitive})\\
Coefficientwise, countable scales & Absolutely convergent real sums at every
exponent & Yes & Not on a countably infinite sample space\\
Ultrafilter or numerosity & Finite additivity, with a separately chosen
generalized-limit structure & May recover selected real measurements; no
automatic $\sigma$-additivity & Yes\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}

The table is not a ranking: the right framework depends on whether ordinary
diffuse integration, exact finite counting, geometric measure on a
non-Archimedean domain, or another feature comes first, and no construction makes
these choices disappear.

\part{Scope, questions, verification, and provenance}\label{meas:part:scope}

\section{What is settled, and what is not claimed}\label{meas:sec:scope}

\subsection{What the report settles}

\paragraph{Strong class.} On countably separated sample spaces the representation
problem for strong Hahn measures is solved: atomicity and one common support
follow from the axiom (Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:atomic}), the axiom is equivalent to
finite atomicity of every coefficient (Corollary~\ref{meas:cor:coefficient-test}),
and a strong measure is exactly a coefficientwise one with finitely atomic
coefficients (Corollary~\ref{meas:cor:two-axioms}). The extension problem is solved
for coherent cylinder data over finite alphabets (Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:cylinder})
and for independent finite-state coordinates
(Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:independent}), and the moment problem on ordinary real sample
sets (Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:momentcriterion}). Positivity is a separate
condition in each: the least support order type at which positivity of cylinder
tests or of polynomial squares can fail to imply positivity of events is
$\omega+1$ (Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:threshold}), attained while the coefficient
measures use only one or two nodes per scale. Several independent positivity tests
survive the interval example: pointwise polynomial positivity, all Hermitian and
localizing Hankel forms, all Hausdorff differences, and ordinary continuous
functions; they quantify over every finite degree and every Hahn coefficient
choice, and the obstruction is exposed by reconstructing one discontinuous event,
not by a missed high-degree polynomial. The finite Gaussian theory stays intact
because it is algebraic and admits nonreal nodes.

\paragraph{Coefficientwise class.} Finite-dimensional consistency is not enough:
consistent positive laws with bounded signed coefficient variations need not have
a positive Borel extension (Example~\ref{meas:ex:negative}). For independent
perturbations on a common well-ordered exponent set the exact sequence-space
conditions are identified, and positivity is proved rather than assumed, by
domination in the interior and by nonnegative exact path weights at the
boundary (Theorems~\ref{meas:thm:interior}--\ref{meas:thm:mixed}).

\paragraph{Countably many scales (source 16).} With a countable exponent set, and
finite real coefficient variation, the class has a coherent measure theory with
actual surreal values and diffuse shadows: one real control and a Hahn-valued
density making positivity pointwise, a Hahn--Jordan decomposition, a faithful
density-adapted integral with an exact Radon--Nikodym correspondence, Lebesgue
decomposition, products and Fubini, and a subclass with stable conditioning. The
boundary is equally exact: order smallness does not control coefficient
integrability (Examples~\ref{meas:cw:ex:bounded-fail}
and~\ref{meas:cw:ex:bct-fail}), coefficientwise limits of positive measures can be
negative (Example~\ref{meas:cw:ex:positive-not-closed}), conditioning can require
cancellation before integration (Example~\ref{meas:cw:ex:conditional-fail}), and
the control cannot survive uncountably many scales
(Example~\ref{meas:cw:ex:omega1}).

\paragraph{A practical rule (source 15).} A model treating Hahn coefficients as
finite ordinary measures can carry infinitely many independent infinitesimal
perturbations, but the amplitudes must decay in the sequence space appropriate
to the baseline; a constant nonzero infinitesimal correction repeated at every
coordinate does not decay, and no smaller valuation scale repairs it. A model
insisting on strong addition across every disjoint countable family pays more:
each coefficient becomes finitely atomic, diffuse leading laws are excluded, and
symmetry forces the atoms onto finite orbits. Strong countable additivity is
therefore unsuitable as a direct replacement for ordinary additivity in diffuse
stochastic processes, though it is consistent and useful for scale-separated
atomic models such as $p_j=t^{j\alpha}$. Neither conclusion decrees a unique
convention for surreal probability.

are retained. Statements about the absence of Lean verification describe the
source deliveries; the current repository coverage is summarized in the
formalization subsection below and tracked in \path{docs/FORMALIZATION.md}.

16.1 & Not a measure on the proper class of all surreals; no proper class is used
as a $\sigma$-algebra, support or integration domain; no Borel structure on $\No$ is
assumed. & Conv.~\ref{meas:cw:conv:notation}, Secs.~\ref{meas:cw:sec:probability},
\ref{meas:cw:sec:boundary}\\
16.2 & Not a canonical length measure on every surreal interval; a real-parameter
chart is not the surreal interval; chart consistency must be checked; no canonical
volume on all surreal subsets follows. & Sec.~\ref{meas:cw:sec:boundary}\\
16.3 & Not a blanket extension of the real convergence theorems; its convergence
theorems concern coefficientwise, not surreal-order, limits; no universal Tonelli
theorem; monotone convergence does not cover arbitrary order-monotone Hahn
functions. & Secs.~\ref{meas:cw:sec:rn},~\ref{meas:cw:sec:products}\\
16.4 & The countable-scale and finite-coefficient restrictions are substantive: no
arbitrary $\sigma$-finite theory on the surreal line, no Lebesgue measure on $\R$ in
the class, no uncountable-scale Radon--Nikodym theory. &
Secs.~\ref{meas:cw:sec:control},~\ref{meas:cw:sec:boundary}\\
16.5 & Coefficientwise summation is transported along a specified normal-form
embedding and is not full-surreal convergence; no group is identified with
surreals without a specified embedding. & Conv.~\ref{meas:cw:conv:notation}\\
16.6 & The strong/coefficientwise distinction, strong atomicity, the null-ideal
criterion and the two-scale obstruction are this report's, not source~16's; some of
its arguments reformulate standard scalar measure theory. &
Secs.~\ref{meas:sub:provenance},~\ref{meas:cw:sec:control}\\
16.7 & Its repository inspection was not an exhaustive audit (index, this report's
guide and the opening of its source only). & Sec.~\ref{meas:sec:audit}\\
16.8 & The adapted $\Kc$-valued norm does not make $\Lad(\mu)$ a real Banach space;
order-bounded functions do not form an integrability ideal. &
Thm.~\ref{meas:cw:thm:integral-properties}\\
16.9 & No global completeness once supports vary. & Sec.~\ref{meas:cw:sec:rn}\\
16.10 & No countably infinite product theorem is inferred from finite products. &
Sec.~\ref{meas:cw:sec:products}\\
16.11 & The Cauchy--Schwarz inequality gives no complete Hilbert space and no
spectral theorem; the surcomplex field has no order. &
Sec.~\ref{meas:cw:sec:products}\\
16.12 & Conditional expectation is not asserted to lie in the original domain; no
single projection on all adapted integrands exists
(Ex.~\ref{meas:cw:ex:conditional-fail}). & Sec.~\ref{meas:cw:sec:probability}\\
16.13 & Borel--Cantelli only under per-coefficient variation summability. &
Prop.~\ref{meas:cw:prop:borel-cantelli}\\
16.14 & Replacing a divergent coefficient by a chosen infinite surreal is a
regularization rule, not a consequence. & Ex.~\ref{meas:cw:ex:bct-fail}\\
16.15 & No claim that surreal scalars regularize a physical singularity. &
Sec.~\ref{meas:cw:sec:boundary}\\
16.16 & The numerosity construction is of a standard type; its infinitesimal
depends on the embedding; its standard-part set function is not countably
additive; it is not to be conflated with the Benci--Horsten--Wenmackers axiom;
standard parts on an arbitrary event algebra are not a Loeb procedure. &
Sec.~\ref{meas:cw:sub:numerosity}\\
16.17 & The uncountable example refutes no theorem; uncountable support needs
further theories. & Ex.~\ref{meas:cw:ex:omega1}\\
16.18 & The framework table is not a ranking; no framework makes the choices
disappear. & Sec.~\ref{meas:cw:sec:boundary}\\
16.19 & Positivity must be checked on the generated $\sigma$-algebra or by a density
certificate; finite tests cannot replace it. & Prop.~\ref{meas:cw:prop:extension}\\
16.20 & The order-limit theorem concerns set-indexed nets only, with a
smallness caveat for universe-relative formalization; selecting simplest surreals
is not a supremum. & Sec.~\ref{meas:cw:sub:orderlimits}\\
16.21 & Leading-scale conditioning resembles lexicographic probability; exact
$\sigma$-additivity is additional structure. & Prop.~\ref{meas:cw:prop:leading}\\
16.22 & Scalar Radon--Nikodym, Fubini, conditional expectation, Carath\'eodory,
Neumann's lemma and the field embedding are classical inputs. &
Secs.~\ref{meas:cw:sec:control},~\ref{meas:cw:sub:numerosity}\\
16.23 & No compiling Lean implementation; the repository's checked results do not
formalize its theorems. & Rem.~\ref{meas:cw:rem:routes},
Sec.~\ref{meas:sec:audit}\\
16.24 & Its finite checks verify no infinite summability, countable additivity,
almost-everywhere statement or infinite divergence; its build record is a record
of delivery, not regenerated. & Sec.~\ref{meas:sec:verification}\\
16.25 & Not independently refereed; no certified novelty or priority, also for the
combination; no published conjecture resolved; the literature comparison is
targeted, and related formulations in the literature are not excluded. & Title
page, Sec.~\ref{meas:sub:literature}\\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\endgroup

Two limitations belong to the merge. The strong spine now has two independent
derivations (sources 10 and 11), but neither was audited against the literature on
vector measures with finite coefficient incidence, so it remains a candidate
contribution. Corollary~\ref{meas:cor:two-axioms} and
Remarks~\ref{meas:rem:mustar-coef} and~\ref{meas:rem:one-exponent} were added in the
merge to reconcile the sources; they are elementary consequences of the sources'
theorems, and they are marked where they appear. The same holds for
Proposition~\ref{meas:cw:prop:nullideal}, Remarks~\ref{meas:cw:rem:routes}
and~\ref{meas:cw:rem:dominated}, Example~\ref{meas:cw:ex:negative-density}, and the
comparisons of source~16's results with Example~\ref{meas:ex:hahn-integrand},
Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:extension} and Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:mixed}, which the
merge added to relate source~16 to the earlier sources. Source~16's own
reliance on classical scalar theorems was checked only against their standard
statements.

\section{Open questions}\label{meas:sec:questions}

The questions below were formulated by the four sources, not taken from a named
list of open problems. Source~16 answers none of the earlier questions; it adds
Questions~\ref{meas:cw:q:conditional} and~\ref{meas:cw:q:uncountable}. Where a question is answered in part, the answer and its
source are recorded and the question stays open, re-scoped.

\begin{question}[Standard Borel alphabets; source 10]\label{meas:q:borel}
Replace the finite alphabets of Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:cylinder} by standard Borel
coordinate spaces. Atomicity still holds (Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:atomic}), but a
finite-width cylinder tree no longer describes the extension problem directly; a
finite atomic compatibility criterion would have to say how point locations are
recovered from the chosen cylinder family. Open.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[A weaker addition rule; source 10]\label{meas:q:weaker}
Find the exact extension theorem for an addition rule that permits convergent
ordinary real coefficient sums, with the correct uniform support and variation
conditions. \emph{Answered in part} by source~15: for coherent data on $E^{\N}$,
$E$ finite, \emph{with one common well-ordered support assumed}, per-exponent
bounded variation is exact (Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:extension}) and the null-ideal
criterion decides positivity (Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:nullideal}). Open without the
common-support assumption, which is source~15's item ``definitions without a
global well-ordered support'' (Question~\ref{meas:q:global}).
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Effective versions; sources 10 and 11]\label{meas:q:effective}
The criteria of Theorems~\ref{meas:thm:cylinder}, \ref{meas:thm:independent}
and~\ref{meas:thm:momentcriterion} are necessary and sufficient mathematical
conditions, not decision procedures on arbitrary coefficient oracles; finite-prefix
verification cannot decide well ordering or eventual width bounds. Which restricted
representations with certificates for these infinite conditions make them
decidable? Open.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Positive functionals with extra continuity; source 11]
\label{meas:q:functionals}
Classify positive functionals on $C(\Omega,\R)$ with Hahn values under continuity
conditions strong enough to recover a positive representing object but weak enough
to include ordinary diffuse probability. Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:atomic} shows that
the strong axiom on all Borel events cannot include diffuse laws. In the
coefficientwise class, per-exponent boundedness of the coefficient functionals gives
a unique signed representing coefficientwise measure (the Riesz--Markov step of
Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:extension}, applied exponent by exponent), and
Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:nullideal} decides whether that measure is positive. But
positivity of the functional does not suffice: the functional $T$ of
Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:example}(iii) is positive on $C([0,1],\R)$ with per-exponent
bounded coefficients, and its representing measure is not positive in either class
(Remark~\ref{meas:rem:mustar-coef}). A continuity condition on the functional alone
that guarantees a positive representing measure is not established. Open,
re-scoped.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Hahn-valued sample points; source 11]\label{meas:q:hahnpoints}
Study measures on Hahn-valued sample points. The finite-coefficient Vandermonde
argument does not directly reduce moments to finite measures at ordinary nodes,
since a nonreal sample point has its own exponent expansion and mixes scales under
powers; the positive quadratures of Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:quadratureresidues} give
concrete data. No general existence or uniqueness claim is made. The collection's
Prony report~\cite{repoprony} treats the different finite problem of reconstructing
a finite weighted configuration with Hahn-valued nodes from its power moments. Open.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Multivariate separation; source 11]\label{meas:q:multivariate}
Which algebraic sets of earlier atoms permit a prescribed later signed tail to remain
positive on a given polynomial cone? Corollary~\ref{meas:cor:multivariate} gives a
sufficient condition only. Open.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Near-boundary baselines; source 15]\label{meas:q:nearboundary}
When $p_j^0$ approaches $0$ or $1$ without reaching it, Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:extension}
remains the exact abstract answer, but the weighted-$\ell^2$ condition of
Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:interior} is no longer necessary
(Example~\ref{meas:ex:near-boundary}). Find an intrinsic sequence-space description
distinguishing rare large scores from uniformly nondegenerate ones and controlling
all nonlinear coefficient terms. Open.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Several vanishing residues; source 15]\label{meas:q:residues}
Extend the coefficientwise thresholds to finite alphabets with several vanishing
residue probabilities. In the strong class this is answered, for arbitrary finite
alphabets and residue patterns, by Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:independent} (source~10).
Open in the coefficientwise class.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Markov dependence; source 15]\label{meas:q:markov}
Replace independence by dependence governed by a transition kernel. Open in both
classes. The collection's report on finite-state Hahn Markov
generators~\cite{repomarkov} excludes path measures and does not treat this
extension problem.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[No global well-ordered support; source 15]\label{meas:q:global}
Find definitions of countable additivity without a global well-ordered support across
all events. For the strong axiom \eqref{meas:eq:SH} the question is answered: on
countably separated spaces the common support is automatic
(Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:atomic}, sources 10 and 11), and even finite coefficient
atomicity alone forces it (Corollary~\ref{meas:cor:coefficient-test}). Open for
coefficientwise and other rules. Source~16's adapted integral
(Definition~\ref{meas:cw:def:adapted}) drops the common well-ordered support for
\emph{integrands}, not for measures, and leaves this question unchanged.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Conditioning-stable coefficient classes; source 16]
\label{meas:cw:q:conditional}
Characterize the largest classes of density coefficients, between the bounded
unit-leading class of Theorem~\ref{meas:cw:thm:unit-conditional} and general
integrable coefficients, on which the conditional quotients
\eqref{meas:cw:eq:conditional-ratio} stay in the original integrable class.
Example~\ref{meas:cw:ex:conditional-fail} shows that integrable coefficients alone
do not suffice. Open.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Beyond countably many scales; source 16]
\label{meas:cw:q:uncountable}
Source~16 names what its theory does not give: a $\sigma$-finite theory (for instance
Lebesgue measure on $\R$ with unchanged lengths), and integration and
Radon--Nikodym theory with uncountably many scales, where no single control exists
(Example~\ref{meas:cw:ex:omega1}) and, by the example of the finite
surreal-probability report cited in Section~\ref{meas:cw:sec:boundary}, positivity of
naive coefficientwise integrals fails. It suggests local controls or transfinite
null ideals (Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:nullideal}). Which of these, if any, gives an
integral with a Radon--Nikodym theorem? (Formulated in the merge from source~16's
stated boundary.) Open.
\end{question}

\section{The four check suites}\label{meas:sec:verification}

Each source delivered a standard-library Python program using exact rational
arithmetic; they are kept byte-identical under \path{code/}, with their recorded
outputs under \path{data/}. They check finite identities that detect algebraic
mistakes. They do not prove any infinite statement, cannot establish that a support
is well ordered or that infinitely many widths are bounded, and certify neither
novelty nor Lean correctness.

\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabularx}{\textwidth}{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.33\textwidth}r>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}X@{}}
\toprule
Program & Assertions & What it checks\\
\midrule
\path{10-strong-hahn-measures-verify.py} & 5{,}135 & The scalar nonvanishing
probability bound behind the randomized selection (30); finite coefficient trees,
width monotonicity and reconstruction (3{,}600); finite independent products and
first-error recovery (408); finite truncations of the Cantor-space pattern,
including that the cutoff reveals the negative atom (1{,}035); the rank-two
coarse-graining coefficient witness $\frac12$ along descending $\gamma_n$ (62).
Exponents are lexicographic rational tuples.\\[3pt]
\path{11-hahn-moments-verify.py} & 682 & Determinants against the Cauchy--Binet
expansion (68) and predicted valuations and leading coefficients (68); square and
localizer positivity for 96 seeded polynomials with finite Hahn coefficients and
four localizers (384); simple-root coefficient recurrences (90); negative detectors
for finite truncations (8) and restoration by the next atom (8); tent-function
leading terms (8); the closed Vandermonde formula through $d=12$ (13);
harmonic-number valuations (13); Jacobi valuation differences (12); normalization
and the negative singleton (10). Rational exponents; seed \texttt{20260922}.\\[3pt]
\path{15-infinite-products-of-probabilities-verify.py} & 1{,}009 & Finite
positivity (186), normalization (15) and marginal consistency (93); the
ordered-word/$(1/k!)$ identity against direct products (128); the multilinear $L^2$
bound (39); first-order variations at interior and boundary baselines (8, 8, 5) and
the two-sided bounds of Lemma~\ref{meas:lem:L1lower} at the fair baseline (8,
recorded under the name \path{interior_moment_bounds}); the cylinders of
Example~\ref{meas:ex:negative} (510) and its normalization (8); and one check
(\path{counterexample_singleton_formula}) that only asserts that a hard-coded
value is negative, so is tautological. Integer exponents only.\\[3pt]
\path{16-coefficientwise-measure-theory-verify_examples.py} & 1{,}722 & Exact
rational series modulo $t^9$, seed \texttt{20260922}, 40 random trials on finite
spaces: normalization (80) and formal inversion (40); finite Radon--Nikodym
identities (280); conditional expectation, total expectation, idempotence, tower,
event and pull-out identities (280); finite Fubini (80) and rectangle mass (40);
Hahn--Jordan reconstruction and attainment (80) and the finite-partition bound of
Theorem~\ref{meas:cw:thm:variation} over all partitions of four points (600); the
coefficients of \eqref{meas:cw:eq:covariance} against series division (80); the
coefficients of Example~\ref{meas:cw:ex:worked} (2); the fibre cancellation,
opposite $t^2$-coefficients and harmonic term of
Example~\ref{meas:cw:ex:conditional-fail} for $n\le40$ (120). The 40 checks named
\path{RN support obstruction} only multiply two hard-coded monomials, so are
tautological. Integer exponents only.\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}

The programs of sources 10, 11 and 15 were rerun on a scratch copy of this
directory when the report was first written (Python 3.14.4). All assertions passed with the recorded counts; the
outputs agree with the delivered records except for the recorded Python version
(source~10) and line endings (source~11). Two of the programs write files, so run
them on a copy: \path{10-strong-hahn-measures-verify.py} writes
\path{verification.json} and \path{verification.txt} into the \path{data/}
directory next to its own \path{code/} directory (unprefixed names, beside the
prefixed records), and \path{11-hahn-moments-verify.py} writes
\path{verification_results.json} and \path{verification_summary.txt} into its
own directory unless \path{--output-dir} is given.
\path{15-infinite-products-of-probabilities-verify.py} prints to standard output
and writes a file only with \path{--output}. \path{16-coefficientwise-measure-theory-verify_examples.py} prints its JSON record
and writes a file only with \path{--output}. The delivered build scripts
(\path{code/*-build.sh}) are kept as delivered; they refer to the source packages'
own layouts and do not build this report. The file
\path{15-infinite-products-of-probabilities-build_summary.json} is source~15's
hand-recorded note on its own 23-page build, and
\path{16-coefficientwise-measure-theory-build_validation.json} is source~16's record
of its own 29-page build; no program generates either.

When source~16 was merged, all four programs were rerun on a scratch copy under
Python 3.14.4. They passed 5{,}135, 682, 1{,}009 and 1{,}722 assertions; source~16's
output agrees with its delivered record except for the recorded Python version.

\paragraph{Maintained review rerun.}
The three unchanged programs were run on temporary copies under Python
3.13.14 during the documentation review. They passed 5,135, 682 and 1,009
assertions respectively. The copied program hashes match the preserved
originals; generated outputs stayed outside the repository. This run has
the finite scope listed above, including the tautological singleton check.

\section{Provenance, audit, and attribution}\label{meas:sec:audit}

\subsection{Repository snapshot and what the sources inspected}

Sources 10, 11 and 15 inspected \repo\ at the snapshot \pin\ (22 September 2026) and
wrote no repository files. Source~10 read the root and catalogue READMEs, directory
listings, and a prior physics text; its code search found no \texttt{Kolmogorov}
match while an API search reported incomplete indexing. Source~11 read the root
README, the catalogue, directory listings and the opening of the spectral-theory
article; its search index returned no hits even for a control term known to occur,
so it treated searches as unusable. Source~15 read the root README, the catalogue,
the opening of \path{docs/FORMALIZATION.md} and directory listings; its indexed
searches for \texttt{Kolmogorov} and \texttt{probability} returned no matches. At
the pin the catalogue listed 26 reports and \path{docs/new} held 18 archives whose
interiors none of the sources could fully read; source~10 named
\path{surreal_symbolic_dynamics.zip} and the infinite-spectral archives as
possible overlaps. Those archives have since been placed as reports of the same
batch as the three sources. A search of the collection made while this report was
written finds no other treatment of Hahn-valued measures than the Herglotz
report~\cite{repoherg}, no occurrence of \texttt{Kolmogorov} outside this report, and
no probability or invariant-measure content in the symbolic-dynamics report
(\path{surcomplex/expanding-polynomial-dynamics}); the infinite-dimensional
spectral report explicitly supplies no spectral measures. The delivered audit
records \path{10-strong-hahn-measures-provenance.md} and
\path{15-infinite-products-of-probabilities-RESEARCH_AUDIT.md} are kept as written
at the pin; source~11's audit was an appendix of its article and is summarized here.

Source~16 was pinned to the later snapshot \pinb. It reports inspecting the
documentation index \path{docs/README.md}, this report's guide and the opening
definitions of this report's source, and says expressly that this was not an audit
of every repository file or of the whole 64-page report. This report's source and
guide did not change between that snapshot and the placement of source~16, so what
it read is the report of sources 10, 11 and 15 as printed here, before the merge.
Its audit is a section of its article and is summarized here and in
Section~\ref{meas:sub:stale}; no separate audit file was delivered.

\subsection{Stale statements corrected}\label{meas:sub:stale}

\begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*)]
\item Source~15 reported that indexed searches for \texttt{probability} returned no
matches. At the pin the physics report already contained
\path{phys:sub:probability} and \path{phys:ex:summable}, on the failure of equal
infinitesimal weights and the need for a summation theory; its scope table lists a
probability theory among things to be supplied, and the spectral-theory report
states that no measure-valued probability theory is supplied. These are cited
(Section~\ref{meas:sub:collection}). Source~15's statement that the catalogue listed
no probability report was correct at the pin; the collection now contains this
report and the Herglotz report.
\item Source~11 reproved the finite spectral theorem over a real closed field; the
collection has it (\path{thm:spectral}, and \path{spec:thm:hermitian} at any
$\Gamma$), and it is cited (Section~\ref{meas:sec:quadrature}).
\item Source~11 found no repository matches for moment-related terms and treated its
index as unusable. The collection does use ``moment'' in other senses (contour,
residue, Schoenberg and inverse moments in other surcomplex reports, and now Fourier
moments in the Herglotz report and finite-configuration power moments in the Prony
report); none is a Hahn-valued measure moment problem
(Convention~\ref{meas:conv:terms}).
\item Source~10 cited the physics discussion as Section 13.3 of a PDF; it is now cited
by its labels in \path{physics/surreal-scalars-and-spacetime}.
\item Source~10's statement that the randomized selection argument is needed for the
coefficientwise criterion is withdrawn (Remark~\ref{meas:rem:selection}).
\item Source~15 did not cite Ludkovsky and Khrennikov; source~10's warning against
``a first non-Archimedean Kolmogorov theorem'' is extended to source~15's extension
theorems (Section~\ref{meas:sub:literature}).
\item Source~16 describes this report as 64 pages, with Theorem~19.2 as its canonical
null-ideal criterion. Both were correct at its pin \pinb; the report now has 86
pages, and Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:nullideal} keeps the number 19.2.
\item Source~16 says that the repository ``has separately recorded checked results for
portions of its existing strong theory''. At its pin the Lean ledger
\path{docs/FORMALIZATION.md} already recorded as proved, besides the strong
atomicity core, the coefficientwise null-ideal criterion
(Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:nullideal}, with its control-measure form) and
Corollary~\ref{meas:cor:dominated}. Its conclusion stands: none of source~16's own
theorems, the density form included, is formalized. This report's own formalization
subsection and guide, which listed only strong-class items, are corrected in the
same way.
\item Source~16 proved the eventual constancy of convergent set-indexed nets from
Conway's cut property and cited Conway; the collection already had it as
\path{found:thm:discrete}, and the absence of suprema as
\path{found:sub:notdedekind}. Both are now cited
(Section~\ref{meas:cw:sub:orderlimits}). Its citation of the documentation index for
the distinction between formal summation and topological convergence is correct.
\item Source~16 treated equal infinitesimal point masses without citing the physics
report's prior observation \path{phys:ex:summable}; the observation is credited to
that report (Section~\ref{meas:cw:sub:numerosity}, non-claim 10.13).
\item Source~16 could not refer to \path{surreal/finite-surreal-probability}, placed in
the same delivery; its uncountable-support integration example is now cited
(Section~\ref{meas:cw:sec:boundary}).
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{A formalization path}

The repository ledger \path{docs/FORMALIZATION.md} records checked
versions of the disjoint-finite scalar lemma, finite coefficient atomicity,
automatic common support, the forward atomic representation and coefficient
criterion, and the construction, integration and positivity of strong atomic
measures. It also records positive-support product prerequisites, and, in the
coefficientwise class, the null-ideal criterion (Theorem~\ref{meas:thm:nullideal},
with the control-measure form) and Corollary~\ref{meas:cor:dominated} as proved
(\path{Surreal/HahnSeries/NullIdealPositivity.lean}). These
declarations have their own precise scope; they do not formalize every
extension, moment, quadrature or coefficientwise theorem here, and nothing in
Sections~\ref{meas:cw:sub:orderlimits} or~\ref{meas:cw:sec:control}--\ref{meas:cw:sec:boundary}
is formalized.

The source's proposed order remains useful for the pending steps: the
coefficient tree lemma from finite branching, monotone natural-number widths
and stabilization of bounded monotone sequences; then cylinder extension,
rare-entry recovery and the independent-coordinate criterion. Track their
dependencies on coefficient maps, strong-family regrouping, positive-support
word finiteness and geometric inversion explicitly.
Source~15's dependency order for the coefficientwise class is: scalar signed-measure
extension; finite-product coefficient identities; the multilinear norm bounds;
common-support Hahn assembly; and the separate positivity arguments.
Source~16's order for the countable-scale class is: common-support Hahn algebra and
the finite-convolution support lemma; countable families of finite signed measures
and the control \eqref{meas:cw:eq:control}; measurable leading-sign strata and
Lemma~\ref{meas:cw:lem:sign}; representation, completion and Hahn--Jordan
decomposition; weighted $L^1$ numerator classes, control invariance and the
Radon--Nikodym correspondence; products and Fubini, then restricted-space
conditioning; finally the bounded unit-leading class. It advises defining a new
field-valued measure object rather than substituting $\No$ for the extended
nonnegative reals of an existing scalar measure structure, and checking the reused
library interfaces at implementation time. The null-ideal declarations above
formalize the coefficient-by-coefficient positivity criterion, not the sign lemma
or the density.

\section{Reproducing the document and the checks}\label{meas:sec:reproduce}

The directory contains this standalone \LaTeX{} source with an embedded
bibliography, its compiled PDF, a README, the four delivered programs and build
scripts under \path{code/}, their recorded outputs and build records under
\path{data/}, and the two delivered audit records. No source manuscript is
redistributed. From a copy of the directory:
\begin{verbatim}
latexmk -pdf -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error article.tex
python code/10-strong-hahn-measures-verify.py
python code/11-hahn-moments-verify.py --output-dir rerun-11
python code/15-infinite-products-of-probabilities-verify.py \
    --output rerun-15.json
python code/16-coefficientwise-measure-theory-verify_examples.py \
    --output rerun-16.json
\end{verbatim}
Python 3.10 or later suffices; only the standard library is used.
